```
1	//! `Highlight` component — text substring matching with Unicode-aware case folding.
2	//!
3	//! `Highlight` is a stateless utility that splits a text into alternating
4	//! highlighted / non-highlighted chunks based on one or more search queries.
5	//! It supports three match strategies (`Contains`, `StartsWith`, `Fuzzy`),
6	//! Unicode-aware case folding via ICU4X `CaseMapper` when the `i18n` feature
7	//! is enabled (locale-aware lowercasing handles Turkic dotted/dotless I,
8	//! German eszett, Greek final sigma, etc.), and multi-query overlap merging.
9	//!
10	//! The component has no state machine. Adapters render each returned
11	//! `HighlightChunk` as a `<mark>` (`highlighted == true`) or `<span>`
12	//! (`highlighted == false`). The agnostic-core consists of `Props`,
13	//! `MatchStrategy`, `HighlightChunk`, the `Part` enum, the `Api` attribute
14	//! mapper, and the `highlight_chunks` standalone computation.
15	//!
16	//! See `spec/components/utility/highlight.md` for the authoritative contract.
17	
18	use alloc::{borrow::ToOwned, string::String, vec, vec::Vec};
19	
20	use ars_core::{AttrMap, ComponentPart, ConnectApi, HtmlAttr};
21	use ars_i18n::Locale;
22	
23	/// Props for the [`Highlight`](crate::utility::highlight) component.
24	///
25	/// `Highlight` does not carry a DOM `id` — chunk output is a sequence of
26	/// adapter-rendered children, not a single identifiable element — so [`Props`]
27	/// does not derive [`HasId`](ars_core::HasId) like other stateless utilities.
28	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
29	pub struct Props {
30	    /// The search queries to highlight. Supports a single query string or
31	    /// multiple queries to highlight simultaneously (e.g., multiple search
32	    /// terms). When multiple queries are provided, all matches from all
33	    /// queries are highlighted; overlapping ranges are merged into a single
34	    /// highlighted chunk. An empty vec, or a vec where every entry is empty,
35	    /// produces a single non-highlighted chunk containing the full text.
36	    pub query: Vec<String>,
37	
38	    /// The full text to search within.
39	    pub text: String,
40	
41	    /// When `true` (default), matching is case-insensitive via Unicode case
42	    /// folding — `ars_i18n::to_lowercase` with the [`Locale`] passed to
43	    /// [`highlight_chunks`] / [`Api::chunks`]. When `false`, byte-exact
44	    /// matching is used (no normalisation pass; the locale parameter is
45	    /// ignored).
46	    pub ignore_case: bool,
47	
48	    /// The strategy used to match the query against the text. Defaults to
49	    /// [`MatchStrategy::Contains`].
50	    pub match_strategy: MatchStrategy,
51	}
52	
53	impl Default for Props {
54	    fn default() -> Self {
55	        Self {
56	            query: Vec::new(),
57	            text: String::new(),
58	            ignore_case: true,
59	            match_strategy: MatchStrategy::Contains,
60	        }
61	    }
62	}
63	
64	impl Props {
65	    /// Returns fresh [`Props`] with the documented defaults — equivalent
66	    /// to [`Default::default`], offered as the entry point for the
67	    /// builder chain.
68	    #[must_use]
69	    pub fn new() -> Self {
70	        Self::default()
71	    }
72	
73	    /// Sets the query list. Accepts any iterable of `Into<String>`, so
74	    /// callers can pass `["foo"]`, `vec!["foo".to_string(), "bar".into()]`,
75	    /// `&["foo", "bar"][..]`, etc.
76	    #[must_use]
77	    pub fn query<I, S>(mut self, query: I) -> Self
78	    where
79	        I: IntoIterator<Item = S>,
80	        S: Into<String>,
81	    {
82	        self.query = query.into_iter().map(Into::into).collect();
83	        self
84	    }
85	
86	    /// Sets the text to search within.
87	    #[must_use]
88	    pub fn text(mut self, text: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
89	        self.text = text.into();
90	        self
91	    }
92	
93	    /// Sets the case-sensitivity flag.
94	    #[must_use]
95	    pub const fn ignore_case(mut self, value: bool) -> Self {
96	        self.ignore_case = value;
97	        self
98	    }
99	
100	    /// Sets the match strategy.
101	    #[must_use]
102	    pub const fn match_strategy(mut self, value: MatchStrategy) -> Self {
103	        self.match_strategy = value;
104	        self
105	    }
106	}
107	
108	/// The strategy used to match the query against the text.
109	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
110	pub enum MatchStrategy {
111	    /// Highlight every occurrence of the query within the text. The default
112	    /// — the most permissive strategy, matching Ark UI's default.
113	    #[default]
114	    Contains,
115	    /// Highlight only the leading run of the text when it starts with the
116	    /// query (after case folding, if `ignore_case` is `true`).
117	    StartsWith,
118	    /// Highlight each individual character of the query within the text in
119	    /// order, allowing arbitrary gaps. Equivalent to a subsequence match
120	    /// (rather than substring). Useful for fuzzy-search experiences.
121	    Fuzzy,
122	}
123	
124	/// One contiguous run of the original text, tagged as highlighted or not.
125	///
126	/// `text` borrows from [`Props::text`] (the lifetime is tied to the props
127	/// passed to [`highlight_chunks`] / [`Api::chunks`]); the chunk vector is
128	/// owned by the caller. Adapters render highlighted chunks as `<mark>` and
129	/// non-highlighted chunks as `<span>` per the component anatomy.
130	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
131	pub struct HighlightChunk<'a> {
132	    /// The slice of the source text covered by this chunk.
133	    pub text: &'a str,
134	    /// Whether the adapter should wrap this chunk in `<mark>`.
135	    pub highlighted: bool,
136	}
137	
138	/// DOM parts of the `Highlight` component.
139	///
140	/// Only [`Part::Root`] is a static anatomy slot. The per-chunk
141	/// `<mark>` / `<span>` elements are emitted dynamically per
142	/// [`HighlightChunk`], parametric on the runtime `highlighted` boolean — a
143	/// value that [`ConnectApi::part_attrs`] (which takes a `Part` by value, no
144	/// payload) cannot express. Adapters call [`Api::chunk_attrs`] per chunk
145	/// instead. See `spec/components/utility/highlight.md` §2 for the rendered
146	/// shape.
147	#[derive(ComponentPart)]
148	#[scope = "highlight"]
149	pub enum Part {
150	    /// The root `<span>` that wraps the chunk children.
151	    Root,
152	}
153	
154	/// Attribute-mapper API for the `Highlight` component.
155	///
156	/// Constructed via [`Api::new`] from [`Props`] and queried via
157	/// [`Api::root_attrs`] / [`Api::chunk_attrs`] for adapter-rendered DOM
158	/// attributes, or [`Api::chunks`] / the standalone [`highlight_chunks`]
159	/// function for the chunk sequence itself.
160	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
161	pub struct Api {
162	    props: Props,
163	}
164	
165	impl Api {
166	    /// Wraps the given props in an `Api`. The props are stored verbatim and
167	    /// can be read back via [`Api::props`] or the typed accessors.
168	    #[must_use]
169	    pub const fn new(props: Props) -> Self {
170	        Self { props }
171	    }
172	
173	    /// Returns a reference to the underlying [`Props`].
174	    #[must_use]
175	    pub const fn props(&self) -> &Props {
176	        &self.props
177	    }
178	
179	    /// Returns the configured query list.
180	    #[must_use]
181	    pub fn query(&self) -> &[String] {
182	        &self.props.query
183	    }
184	
185	    /// Returns the configured source text.
186	    #[must_use]
187	    pub fn text(&self) -> &str {
188	        &self.props.text
189	    }
190	
191	    /// Returns whether matching is case-insensitive.
192	    #[must_use]
193	    pub const fn ignore_case(&self) -> bool {
194	        self.props.ignore_case
195	    }
196	
197	    /// Returns the configured match strategy.
198	    #[must_use]
199	    pub const fn match_strategy(&self) -> MatchStrategy {
200	        self.props.match_strategy
201	    }
202	
203	    /// Attributes for the root `<span>` wrapper.
204	    ///
205	    /// Emits `data-ars-scope="highlight"` + `data-ars-part="root"` (via
206	    /// `Part::Root.data_attrs()`). No ARIA attributes — the semantic
207	    /// `<mark>` elements in the chunk children carry the highlighting
208	    /// semantics for assistive technology.
209	    #[must_use]
210	    pub fn root_attrs(&self) -> AttrMap {
211	        let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();
212	        let [(scope_attr, scope_val), (part_attr, part_val)] = Part::Root.data_attrs();
213	
214	        attrs.set(scope_attr, scope_val).set(part_attr, part_val);
215	
216	        attrs
217	    }
218	
219	    /// Attributes for one chunk element.
220	    ///
221	    /// Emits `data-ars-part="highlight-chunk"` and
222	    /// `data-ars-highlighted="true"|"false"` per `highlighted`. The
223	    /// underlying element (`<mark>` vs `<span>`) is chosen by the adapter
224	    /// based on the same boolean.
225	    #[must_use]
226	    pub fn chunk_attrs(&self, highlighted: bool) -> AttrMap {
227	        let mut attrs = AttrMap::new();
228	
229	        attrs
230	            .set(HtmlAttr::Data("ars-part"), "highlight-chunk")
231	            .set(
232	                HtmlAttr::Data("ars-highlighted"),
233	                if highlighted { "true" } else { "false" },
234	            );
235	
236	        attrs
237	    }
238	
239	    /// Computes the chunk sequence for the configured props at the given
240	    /// locale. Thin convenience around [`highlight_chunks`] — the standalone
241	    /// function takes the same arguments and is the spec-defined entry point.
242	    #[must_use]
243	    pub fn chunks<'a>(&'a self, locale: &Locale) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>> {
244	        highlight_chunks(&self.props, locale)
245	    }
246	}
247	
248	impl ConnectApi for Api {
249	    type Part = Part;
250	
251	    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Part) -> AttrMap {
252	        match part {
253	            Part::Root => self.root_attrs(),
254	        }
255	    }
256	}
257	
258	/// Splits the configured text into alternating highlighted / non-highlighted
259	/// chunks under the given locale.
260	///
261	/// When `props.ignore_case` is `true`, matching uses
262	/// [`ars_i18n::to_lowercase`] for Unicode case folding (under the `i18n`
263	/// feature; falls back to [`str::to_lowercase`] otherwise) so that
264	/// locale-sensitive case pairs match correctly — Turkish dotted/dotless I
265	/// (`İ`/`i` vs `I`/`ı`), German eszett (`ß`/`SS`), and other
266	/// locale-specific mappings handled by the underlying ICU4X `CaseMapper`.
267	/// When `props.ignore_case` is `false`, matching is byte-exact and the
268	/// locale parameter is ignored.
269	///
270	/// When multiple queries match overlapping or adjacent regions, the ranges
271	/// are merged into a single highlighted chunk so adapters never render
272	/// back-to-back `<mark>` elements covering contiguous text.
273	///
274	/// # Edge cases
275	///
276	/// - Empty `query`, or `query` containing only empty strings → a single
277	///   non-highlighted chunk wrapping the full text.
278	/// - Empty `text` → an empty `Vec`.
279	/// - Query longer than text → a single non-highlighted chunk.
280	#[must_use]
281	pub fn highlight_chunks<'a>(props: &'a Props, locale: &Locale) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>> {
282	    let text = props.text.as_str();
283	
284	    if text.is_empty() {
285	        return Vec::new();
286	    }
287	
288	    let non_empty_queries: Vec<&str> = props
289	        .query
290	        .iter()
291	        .map(String::as_str)
292	        .filter(|q| !q.is_empty())
293	        .collect();
294	
295	    if non_empty_queries.is_empty() {
296	        return vec![HighlightChunk {
297	            text,
298	            highlighted: false,
299	        }];
300	    }
301	
302	    let (normalised_text, byte_map) = build_normalised(text, props.ignore_case, locale);
303	
304	    let mut ranges: Vec<(usize, usize)> = Vec::new();
305	
306	    for query in &non_empty_queries {
307	        let normalised_query = if props.ignore_case {
308	            locale_aware_lowercase(query, locale)
309	        } else {
310	            (*query).to_owned()
311	        };
312	
313	        if normalised_query.is_empty() {
314	            continue;
315	        }
316	
317	        match props.match_strategy {
318	            MatchStrategy::Contains => {
319	                push_contains_matches(&normalised_text, &normalised_query, &byte_map, &mut ranges);
320	            }
321	            MatchStrategy::StartsWith => {
322	                push_starts_with_match(&normalised_text, &normalised_query, &byte_map, &mut ranges);
323	            }
324	            MatchStrategy::Fuzzy => {
325	                push_fuzzy_matches(
326	                    text,
327	                    &normalised_query,
328	                    props.ignore_case,
329	                    locale,
330	                    &mut ranges,
331	                );
332	            }
333	        }
334	    }
335	
336	    if ranges.is_empty() {
337	        return vec![HighlightChunk {
338	            text,
339	            highlighted: false,
340	        }];
341	    }
342	
343	    let merged = merge_ranges(ranges);
344	
345	    build_chunks(text, &merged)
346	}
347	
348	/// Build a normalised form of `text` (lowercased when `ignore_case`) plus a
349	/// byte-map from normalised byte indices back to original byte indices.
350	///
351	/// The map has length `normalised.len() + 1`. For each normalised byte `i`
352	/// produced from original char starting at `orig_byte`, `byte_map[i] ==
353	/// orig_byte`. The trailing entry `byte_map[normalised.len()] == text.len()`
354	/// is a sentinel so callers can read the end index of a match without
355	/// bounds-special-casing.
356	///
357	/// When `!ignore_case`, the normalised text is `text` verbatim and the map
358	/// is the identity `0..=text.len()` (each byte trivially maps to itself).
359	fn build_normalised(text: &str, ignore_case: bool, locale: &Locale) -> (String, Vec<usize>) {
360	    if !ignore_case {
361	        let map: Vec<usize> = (0..=text.len()).collect();
362	
363	        return (text.to_owned(), map);
364	    }
365	
366	    let mut normalised = String::with_capacity(text.len());
367	    let mut byte_map = Vec::with_capacity(text.len() + 1);
368	
369	    for (orig_byte, ch) in text.char_indices() {
370	        let mut buf = [0u8; 4];
371	        let ch_str = ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf);
372	        let lowered = locale_aware_lowercase(ch_str, locale);
373	
374	        for _ in 0..lowered.len() {
375	            byte_map.push(orig_byte);
376	        }
377	
378	        normalised.push_str(&lowered);
379	    }
380	
381	    byte_map.push(text.len());
382	
383	    (normalised, byte_map)
384	}
385	
386	/// Locale-aware lowercase. Delegates to `ars_i18n::to_lowercase` (ICU4X
387	/// `CaseMapper`) under the `i18n` feature; falls back to Rust's stdlib
388	/// Unicode lowercase otherwise. The fallback is locale-independent, so
389	/// Turkic and Greek context-specific mappings are only honoured under the
390	/// `i18n` feature.
391	#[cfg(feature = "i18n")]
392	fn locale_aware_lowercase(text: &str, locale: &Locale) -> String {
393	    ars_i18n::to_lowercase(text, locale)
394	}
395	
396	#[cfg(not(feature = "i18n"))]
397	fn locale_aware_lowercase(text: &str, _locale: &Locale) -> String {
398	    text.to_lowercase()
399	}
400	
401	fn push_contains_matches(
402	    normalised_text: &str,
403	    normalised_query: &str,
404	    byte_map: &[usize],
405	    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,
406	) {
407	    for (start, _) in normalised_text.match_indices(normalised_query) {
408	        let end = start + normalised_query.len();
409	        let orig_start = byte_map[start];
410	        let orig_end = byte_map[end];
411	
412	        if orig_start < orig_end {
413	            out.push((orig_start, orig_end));
414	        }
415	    }
416	}
417	
418	fn push_starts_with_match(
419	    normalised_text: &str,
420	    normalised_query: &str,
421	    byte_map: &[usize],
422	    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,
423	) {
424	    if normalised_text.starts_with(normalised_query) {
425	        let end = normalised_query.len();
426	        let orig_end = byte_map[end];
427	
428	        if orig_end > 0 {
429	            out.push((0, orig_end));
430	        }
431	    }
432	}

434	/// Walk the original text char-by-char, lowercasing each char individually
435	/// when needed, and consume the query in order. Each matched original-text

438	fn push_fuzzy_matches(

441	    ignore_case: bool,
442	    locale: &Locale,
443	    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,
444	) {
445	    let mut query_iter = normalised_query.chars().peekable();
446	    let mut local = Vec::new();
447	
448	    for (orig_byte, ch) in original_text.char_indices() {
449	        let Some(&want) = query_iter.peek() else {
450	            break;

474	/// Sort ranges by start and merge overlapping or adjacent ones (`c <= b`).
475	fn merge_ranges(mut ranges: Vec<(usize, usize)>) -> Vec<(usize, usize)> {
476	    ranges.sort_by_key(|&(start, _)| start);
477	
478	    let mut merged: Vec<(usize, usize)> = Vec::with_capacity(ranges.len());
479	
480	    for (start, end) in ranges {
481	        if let Some(last) = merged.last_mut()
482	            && start <= last.1
483	        {
484	            if end > last.1 {
485	                last.1 = end;
486	            }
487	        } else {
488	            merged.push((start, end));
489	        }
490	    }
491	
492	    merged
493	}
494	
495	/// Walk the merged ranges and emit alternating non-highlighted / highlighted
496	/// chunks borrowing from `text`. Zero-length segments are skipped.
497	fn build_chunks<'a>(text: &'a str, ranges: &[(usize, usize)]) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>> {
498	    let mut chunks = Vec::with_capacity(ranges.len() * 2 + 1);
499	    let mut cursor = 0usize;
500	
501	    for &(start, end) in ranges {
502	        if start > cursor {
503	            chunks.push(HighlightChunk {
504	                text: &text[cursor..start],
505	                highlighted: false,
506	            });
507	        }
508	
509	        if end > start {
510	            chunks.push(HighlightChunk {
511	                text: &text[start..end],
512	                highlighted: true,
513	            });
514	        }
515	
516	        cursor = end;
517	    }
518	
519	    if cursor < text.len() {
520	        chunks.push(HighlightChunk {
521	            text: &text[cursor..],
522	            highlighted: false,
523	        });
524	    }
525	
526	    chunks
527	}

# Spec ↔ Implementation Drift Audit

Read both files end-to-end and traced every public surface. Findings below are grouped by severity. The "Best outcome" column ignores which side is currently right and picks the design I'd want if I were starting clean.

---

## High-severity drift

### H1 — `Api` / `ConnectApi` exists despite spec opting out

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | §1.2: *"Highlight uses a function-based API that returns text chunks **rather than** the standard `ConnectApi` / `AttrMap` pattern."* |
| Impl does | Provides `Part::Root`, `pub struct Api { props }`, `Api::root_attrs`, `Api::chunk_attrs(bool)`, `Api::chunks(locale)`, `impl ConnectApi for Api`, plus accessor methods. |
| Why it drifted | Issue acceptance criteria said *"Snapshot tests cover the component's `connect()` / `Api` `AttrMap` output for every anatomy part"* — pure-function form has no AttrMap to snapshot. The stateless-tier template (`foundation/10` §4.1.2) also requires `Part` + `Api`. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, fix spec.** A small `Api` is genuinely useful here: it gives adapters one canonical source for `data-ars-scope` / `data-ars-part` / `data-ars-highlighted` strings instead of inlining them. Spec §1.2's "rather than" language is wrong — the right framing is *"function-based output for chunks, plus a thin attribute mapper for the root and per-chunk data attributes."* Rewrite §1.2 to show both. |

### H2 — Anatomy table lists `Chunk` as a part, `Part` enum doesn't

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | §2 anatomy table has two rows: `Root` and `Chunk`. |
| Impl does | `Part::Root` only. `Part::Chunk` is intentionally absent because chunk attrs depend on a runtime bool, and `ConnectApi::part_attrs(Part)` takes the variant by value with no payload. The dynamic slot is served by `Api::chunk_attrs(bool)`. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, refine spec.** Anatomy parts come in two shapes: *static slots* (one DOM element per component instance, expressible as a `Part` variant) and *parametric slots* (zero-to-N elements per instance, parameterised on runtime data). `Chunk` is the latter. Spec §2 should keep the row but annotate it: *"Chunk is a parametric anatomy slot — its attributes are produced by `Api::chunk_attrs(highlighted: bool)` rather than via the static `Part` enum."* This is a pattern that will recur (table rows, virtualised lists, tags-input chips) so naming it in `foundation/10` would pay off across the catalog. |

---

## Medium-severity drift

### M1 — Adjacent ranges are merged in the core, spec puts that on adapters

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | §3.1: *"For fuzzy matches with many small highlights, **adapters SHOULD consolidate** adjacent highlighted chunks into a single `<mark>` element..."* |
| Impl does | `merge_ranges` already merges `start <= last.1`, which covers overlap **and** adjacency. Adapters never see back-to-back highlighted chunks in the first place. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, move the contract into the core in the spec.** Doing the consolidation in the core is strictly better: (a) screen readers don't hear a stutter of "highlighted, highlighted, highlighted" for adjacent fuzzy hits, (b) DOM output is leaner, (c) every adapter gets the right behaviour for free. Spec §3.1 should change from *"adapters SHOULD"* to *"the agnostic core consolidates adjacent and overlapping highlighted ranges before returning; adapters MAY layer additional summary announcements on top for high-cardinality fuzzy results."* |

### M2 — `i18n` feature flag is invisible to the spec

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | §1.2: *"The implementation MUST use locale-aware Unicode case mapping rather than ASCII `to_lowercase()`."* No mention of build features. |
| Impl does | Gated on `cfg(feature = "i18n")`; falls back to `str::to_lowercase` (Unicode-aware, locale-independent) when the feature is off. The Turkic / German / Greek guarantees only hold with `i18n` on. |
| Why it drifted | `ars-components` declares `ars-i18n = { default-features = false }`, so `ars_i18n::to_lowercase` isn't reachable without an explicit feature. Adapters need to support both `icu4x` and `web-intl` backends; the spec contract for Turkic was written as if locale-aware was free. |
| **Best outcome** | **Make `i18n` always on for `ars-components`, drop the fallback.** Reasons: (a) the spec contract talks in absolutes ("MUST"), so the fallback is a quiet contract violation; (b) every adapter on the roadmap pulls in ICU4X via its own default features anyway — nobody is shipping `ars-components` without it; (c) keeping the fallback doubles the code path surface (untested branch in the no-feature build is the silent failure waiting to happen). Mechanism: change `ars-components/Cargo.toml` to `ars-i18n = { ..., features = ["icu4x"] }` *only* when web-intl isn't selected, OR make the highlight module itself gated on `i18n` so the contract is "if you use Highlight, you've enabled `i18n`." The second is cleaner; the first conflicts with web-intl mutual exclusion. |

### M3 — `Default` impl, `Eq` derive, and `MatchStrategy::Contains` default are spec-invisible

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | `#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]` on `Props`; `#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]` on `MatchStrategy`. Props default values described in prose only (`"default: true"` for `ignore_case`). |
| Impl does | Adds `impl Default for Props`, `#[derive(Default)]` + `#[default] Contains` on `MatchStrategy`, `Eq` on `MatchStrategy` and `HighlightChunk`. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, lift defaults into the spec.** `Default` is the right way to encode the *"default: true"* prose — it makes the default machine-checkable. `Contains` is the right default (most permissive, matches Ark UI). `Eq` on `MatchStrategy` and `HighlightChunk` is free (no float fields) and helps test code use `assert_eq!` without thinking. The spec should derive `Default` + `Eq` on the same items and replace the prose default with a `Default` impl. |

### M4 — Builder pattern is impl-only

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | Nothing. |
| Impl does | `Props::new().query(["foo"]).text("...").ignore_case(false).match_strategy(MatchStrategy::Fuzzy)`. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, show the builder in spec §1.1.** Builders are the workspace convention for stateless props (separator, visually-hidden, focus_ring all have them); the spec template even allows them in §4.1.2. Surfacing the builder in the Highlight spec keeps the catalog consistent and saves adapter authors a roundtrip to read the impl. |

### M5 — Spec adapter example hardcodes data attribute strings

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | §4: `<span data-ars-scope="highlight" data-ars-part="root">` (literal strings). |
| Impl does | Provides `Api::root_attrs()` and `Api::chunk_attrs(bool)` so adapters spread typed attrs. |
| **Best outcome** | **Rewrite spec §4 to use the spread pattern.** If `Api::root_attrs()` exists (per H1), the spec must promote it as the canonical adapter path — otherwise adapters will copy the spec's hardcoded strings and drift when scope or part names change. Replace the literal-string example with `<span ..api.root_attrs()> ... <mark ..api.chunk_attrs(true)>{chunk.text}</mark> ...`. |

---

## Low-severity drift

### L1 — Empty-text behaviour is undefined in spec

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | Function comment specifies empty-query behaviour (one non-highlighted chunk) but is silent on empty-text. |
| Impl does | Empty text → empty `Vec`. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, document explicitly in spec.** `Vec::new()` is the honest answer — there's nothing to render. Returning `vec![HighlightChunk { text: "", highlighted: false }]` would force adapters to filter empty chunks downstream. Spec §1.2 doc-comment should add the empty-text case alongside the empty-query case. |

### L2 — Locale-when-ignore_case-false is undefined in spec

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | Nothing. |
| Impl does | Ignores the locale (and documents that). |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, add one sentence to spec.** Obvious behaviour, but make it visible: *"When `ignore_case` is `false`, the `locale` parameter is ignored — matching is byte-exact."* |

### L3 — Fuzzy "all-or-nothing" semantics are undocumented

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | §1.1 enum doc: *"Highlight individual characters for fuzzy matching."* |
| Impl does | Requires the *entire* query subsequence to be found; if any char is missing, that query contributes zero ranges. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, document.** All-or-nothing is the right choice — partial matches would highlight stray characters that don't represent the query, which is a confusing UX. Spec doc on `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` should say: *"All query characters must appear in order within the text, allowing arbitrary gaps. When the full subsequence is found, each matched character becomes its own one-char highlighted range. When any query character cannot be located, no fuzzy match is emitted for that query."* |

### L4 — Multi-query "overlap" vs "overlap+adjacent" is ambiguous

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | *"overlapping ranges are merged"* (§1.1, §1.2). |
| Impl does | Merges overlap **and** adjacency. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, sharpen spec wording.** Same direction as M1. Spec should say *"overlapping or adjacent ranges"* throughout. |

### L5 — Spec doesn't note absence of `HasId`

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | No `id` field, no comment about it. |
| Impl does | Notes in a doc comment that `Props` doesn't derive `HasId` because the rendered output is a sequence of chunks, not a single addressable element. |
| **Best outcome** | **Keep impl, mention in spec §1.1.** Worth one sentence so adapter authors don't go looking for an `id` field. |

### L6 — BiDi guidance lives only in spec §3.2

| | |
|---|---|
| Spec says | Adapter SHOULD set `dir="auto"` on root and consider `unicode-bidi: isolate` on chunks. |
| Impl does | Nothing — leaves it to adapter. |
| **Best outcome** | **Move part of it into the core.** `dir="auto"` is a static attribute on the root with no downside — `Api::root_attrs()` should emit `dir="auto"` so every adapter gets BiDi-safe behaviour for free, and the spec §3.2 wording would shift from *"adapter SHOULD set `dir="auto"`"* to *"the core sets `dir="auto"` on the root. Adapters MAY add `unicode-bidi: isolate` styling on chunk elements as a layered enhancement."* The `unicode-bidi: isolate` part is a CSS concern that the core can't reach, so leave that on adapters. |

---

## Spec mistakes that aren't drift but should be fixed in the same pass

### X1 — `MatchStrategy` derive list misses `Eq`

`#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]` is what the spec shows. The type is a simple C-like enum with no payload — `Eq` is free and would let downstream code use `==` in `const` contexts and `match` with exhaustive patterns more freely. Same for `HighlightChunk`.

### X2 — `HighlightChunk` derives in spec miss `Eq`

`#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]` — `text: &'a str` and `highlighted: bool` are both `Eq`, so add it.

### X3 — Spec §3.1's screen-reader claim is unsourced

> *"Screen readers announce `<mark>` content as 'highlighted' in most browser/AT combinations."*

This is true for VoiceOver/JAWS in some configurations and false in others (NVDA in default settings doesn't announce `<mark>` at all without ARIA help). The spec's downstream guidance ("no ARIA attributes needed") rests on this claim. Either cite the source (WAI-ARIA-Practices, ARIA in HTML, or a measured AT survey) or soften to *"`<mark>` is the semantic element for highlighted text; AT announcement varies by browser/AT combination."*

---

## Summary of recommended direction

| Where to put truth | Items |
|---|---|
| **Spec changes** (8) | H1, H2, M1, M3, M4, M5, L1, L2, L3, L4, L5, X1, X2, X3 — mostly *spec catches up to impl* + explicit defaults + adapter example uses helpers. |
| **Impl changes** (2) | L6 — add `dir="auto"` to `root_attrs()`; M2 — pick a model for `i18n` and either remove the fallback or properly document it (favour: make Highlight module gated on `i18n` and drop the fallback). |
| **Both** (1) | M2 again — paired Cargo.toml + spec changes describing the build model. |
| **Status quo is correct, just needs documentation** (4) | M3 (defaults), L1, L2, L3 (edge cases), L5 (no HasId). |

Nothing here is a correctness bug; the code does what its tests and acceptance criteria say. The drift is concentrated in two pockets:

1. **Spec wrote "rather than ConnectApi/AttrMap" while the template required ConnectApi/AttrMap.** Impl picked the template + acceptance-criteria side. The right reconciliation is *"the core has a thin attribute mapper for the root and a parameterised helper for chunks, alongside the chunk-computation function"* — and the spec gets a one-paragraph rewrite to say that.
2. **Build-feature gating is invisible to the spec.** The MUST language for ICU4X is currently violated in the `i18n`-off build. Either tighten the impl (Highlight requires `i18n`) or loosen the spec (document the fallback and what it doesn't guarantee). I'd go with tighten.

Want me to land any of these as follow-up edits to the highlight spec/impl before we open the PR, or hold for separate tasks?

{

  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Splits the configured text into alternating highlighted / non-highlighted\n/// chunks under the given locale.\n///\n/// When `props.ignore_case` is `true`, matching uses\n/// [`ars_i18n::to_lowercase`] for Unicode case folding (under the `i18n`\n/// feature; falls back to [`str::to_lowercase`] otherwise) so that\n/// locale-sensitive case pairs match correctly — Turkish dotted/dotless I\n/// (`İ`/`i` vs `I`/`ı`), German eszett (`ß`/`SS`), and other\n/// locale-specific mappings handled by the underlying ICU4X `CaseMapper`.\n/// When `props.ignore_case` is `false`, matching is byte-exact and the\n/// locale parameter is ignored.\n///\n/// When multiple queries match overlapping or adjacent regions, the ranges\n/// are merged into a single highlighted chunk so adapters never render\n/// back-to-back `<mark>` elements covering contiguous text.\n///\n/// # Edge cases\n///\n/// - Empty `query`, or `query` containing only empty strings → a single\n///   non-highlighted chunk wrapping the full text.\n/// - Empty `text` → an empty `Vec`.\n/// - Query longer than text → a single non-highlighted chunk.\n#[must_use]\npub fn highlight_chunks<'a>(props: &'a Props, locale: &Locale) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>> {",
  "new_string": "/// Splits the configured text into alternating highlighted / non-highlighted\n/// chunks under the given locale.\n///\n/// When `props.ignore_case` is `true`, matching uses\n/// [`ars_i18n::to_lowercase`] for locale-aware Unicode case mapping (ICU4X\n/// `CaseMapper::lowercase_to_string` under the hood) so locale-sensitive\n/// case pairs match correctly — Turkish dotted/dotless I (`İ`/`i` vs\n/// `I`/`ı`), German eszett (`ß`/`SS`), Greek final sigma, Lithuanian\n/// dotted I, and other CLDR-supplied mappings. When `props.ignore_case` is\n/// `false`, matching is byte-exact and the `locale` parameter is ignored.\n///\n/// When multiple queries (or fuzzy hits) produce overlapping **or\n/// adjacent** ranges, they are merged into a single highlighted chunk so\n/// adapters never render back-to-back `<mark>` elements covering\n/// contiguous text. This satisfies spec §3.1's adjacency-consolidation\n/// requirement in the core, leaving adapters free to layer only\n/// genuinely additional a11y behaviour (e.g., summary announcements for\n/// high-cardinality fuzzy results) on top.\n///\n/// # Edge cases\n///\n/// - Empty `query`, or `query` containing only empty strings → a single\n///   non-highlighted chunk wrapping the full text.\n/// - Empty `text` → an empty [`Vec`] (nothing to render).\n/// - Query longer than text → a single non-highlighted chunk.\n/// - `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` with any query character missing from the\n///   text → no fuzzy ranges contributed for that query (all-or-nothing).\n#[must_use]\npub fn highlight_chunks<'a>(props: &'a Props, locale: &Locale) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>> {"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs",
  "old_string": "/// The strategy used to match the query against the text.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum MatchStrategy {\n    /// Highlight every occurrence of the query within the text. The default\n    /// — the most permissive strategy, matching Ark UI's default.\n    #[default]\n    Contains,\n    /// Highlight only the leading run of the text when it starts with the\n    /// query (after case folding, if `ignore_case` is `true`).\n    StartsWith,\n    /// Highlight each individual character of the query within the text in\n    /// order, allowing arbitrary gaps. Equivalent to a subsequence match\n    /// (rather than substring). Useful for fuzzy-search experiences.\n    Fuzzy,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// The strategy used to match the query against the text.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub enum MatchStrategy {\n    /// Highlight every occurrence of the query within the text. The default\n    /// — the most permissive strategy, matching Ark UI's default.\n    #[default]\n    Contains,\n    /// Highlight only the leading run of the text when it starts with the\n    /// query (after case folding, if `ignore_case` is `true`).\n    StartsWith,\n    /// Subsequence match: highlight each individual query character within\n    /// the text in order, allowing arbitrary gaps.\n    ///\n    /// All query characters must be present (in order, not necessarily\n    /// contiguous). When the full subsequence is found, each matched\n    /// character contributes its own one-char highlighted range. When any\n    /// query character cannot be located, **no fuzzy ranges are emitted\n    /// for that query** (all-or-nothing) — partial matches would highlight\n    /// characters that don't represent the query and confuse the user.\n    Fuzzy,\n}"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/spec/components/utility/highlight.md",
  "content": "---\ncomponent: Highlight\ncategory: utility\ntier: stateless\nfoundation_deps: [architecture, accessibility]\nshared_deps: []\nrelated: []\nreferences:\n  ark-ui: Highlight\n---\n\n# Highlight\n\n`Highlight` is a stateless utility that splits a text into alternating highlighted / non-highlighted chunks based on one or more search queries. It has no state machine. Adapters render the returned chunks as `<mark>` (highlighted) or `<span>` (non-highlighted) inside a single `<span>` root.\n\n## 1. API\n\n`Highlight` follows the standard stateless utility shape: `Props` + `Part` + `Api`, plus a public free function (`highlight_chunks`) that does the chunk computation. Adapters spread `Api::root_attrs()` / `Api::chunk_attrs(highlighted)` onto the rendered elements and iterate `Api::chunks(locale)` (or call `highlight_chunks` directly) for the chunk sequence.\n\n### 1.1 Props\n\n```rust\n/// Props for the `Highlight` component.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct Props {\n    /// The search queries to highlight. Supports a single query string or\n    /// multiple queries to highlight simultaneously (e.g., multiple search\n    /// terms). When multiple queries are provided, all matches from all\n    /// queries are highlighted; overlapping or adjacent ranges are merged\n    /// into a single highlighted chunk. An empty vec, or a vec containing\n    /// only empty strings, produces a single non-highlighted chunk\n    /// wrapping the full text. Default: empty.\n    pub query: Vec<String>,\n    /// The full text to search within. Default: empty.\n    pub text: String,\n    /// Case-insensitive matching. Default: `true`.\n    pub ignore_case: bool,\n    /// Matching strategy. Default: `MatchStrategy::Contains`.\n    pub match_strategy: MatchStrategy,\n}\n\nimpl Default for Props {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            query: Vec::new(),\n            text: String::new(),\n            ignore_case: true,\n            match_strategy: MatchStrategy::Contains,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// The strategy used to match the query against the text.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub enum MatchStrategy {\n    /// Highlight every occurrence of the query within the text. Default.\n    #[default]\n    Contains,\n    /// Highlight only the leading run of the text when it starts with the\n    /// query (after case folding, if `ignore_case` is `true`).\n    StartsWith,\n    /// Subsequence match: highlight each individual query character within\n    /// the text in order, allowing arbitrary gaps. All query characters\n    /// must be present; if any character is missing, no fuzzy ranges are\n    /// emitted for that query (all-or-nothing).\n    Fuzzy,\n}\n```\n\n`Highlight` does **not** derive `HasId` and has no `id` field — the rendered output is a sequence of chunks rather than a single addressable DOM element.\n\nA fluent builder is provided per the workspace stateless-utility convention (see `foundation/10-component-spec-template.md` §4.1.2):\n\n```rust\nlet props = Props::new()\n    .query([\"foo\", \"bar\"])              // accepts any IntoIterator<Item = Into<String>>\n    .text(\"foobar\")\n    .ignore_case(false)\n    .match_strategy(MatchStrategy::Fuzzy);\n```\n\n### 1.2 Connect / API\n\n```rust\n/// One contiguous run of the original text, tagged as highlighted or not.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub struct HighlightChunk<'a> {\n    /// The slice of the source text covered by this chunk.\n    pub text: &'a str,\n    /// Whether the adapter should wrap this chunk in `<mark>`.\n    pub highlighted: bool,\n}\n\n/// DOM parts of the `Highlight` component.\n///\n/// Only `Root` is a static anatomy slot. The per-chunk `<mark>` / `<span>`\n/// elements are emitted dynamically per `HighlightChunk`, parametric on the\n/// runtime `highlighted` boolean — a value `ConnectApi::part_attrs` (which\n/// takes a `Part` by value, no payload) cannot express. Adapters call\n/// `Api::chunk_attrs(highlighted)` per chunk instead. See §2 Anatomy.\n#[derive(ComponentPart)]\n#[scope = \"highlight\"]\npub enum Part {\n    Root,\n}\n\npub struct Api { props: Props }\n\nimpl Api {\n    pub const fn new(props: Props) -> Self;\n    pub const fn props(&self) -> &Props;\n    pub fn query(&self) -> &[String];\n    pub fn text(&self) -> &str;\n    pub const fn ignore_case(&self) -> bool;\n    pub const fn match_strategy(&self) -> MatchStrategy;\n\n    /// Attributes for the root `<span>`. Emits `data-ars-scope=\"highlight\"`,\n    /// `data-ars-part=\"root\"`, and `dir=\"auto\"` (so the browser picks the\n    /// correct BiDi paragraph direction from the first strong character —\n    /// see §3.2).\n    pub fn root_attrs(&self) -> AttrMap;\n\n    /// Attributes for one chunk element. Emits\n    /// `data-ars-part=\"highlight-chunk\"` and\n    /// `data-ars-highlighted=\"true\"|\"false\"`. The element type (`<mark>`\n    /// vs `<span>`) is chosen by the adapter from the same boolean.\n    pub fn chunk_attrs(&self, highlighted: bool) -> AttrMap;\n\n    /// Convenience around `highlight_chunks`.\n    pub fn chunks<'a>(&'a self, locale: &Locale) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>>;\n}\n\nimpl ConnectApi for Api {\n    type Part = Part;\n    fn part_attrs(&self, part: Part) -> AttrMap;  // Part::Root → root_attrs\n}\n\n/// Splits the text into alternating highlighted / non-highlighted segments.\n///\n/// When `ignore_case` is `true`, matching uses locale-aware Unicode case\n/// mapping via `ars_i18n::to_lowercase` (which wraps ICU4X\n/// `CaseMapper::lowercase_to_string` with the locale's language identifier).\n/// This ensures correct matching for Turkish dotted/dotless I (`İ`/`i` vs\n/// `I`/`ı`), German eszett (`ß`/`SS`), Greek final sigma, Lithuanian\n/// dotted I, and other locale-tailored case mappings supplied by CLDR.\n///\n/// When `ignore_case` is `false`, matching is byte-exact and the `locale`\n/// parameter is ignored.\n///\n/// When multiple queries (or fuzzy hits) produce overlapping or adjacent\n/// ranges, the agnostic core merges them into a single highlighted chunk\n/// before returning — adapters never see back-to-back `<mark>` elements\n/// covering contiguous text.\n///\n/// Edge cases:\n///\n/// - Empty `query`, or `query` containing only empty strings → one\n///   non-highlighted chunk wrapping the full text.\n/// - Empty `text` → an empty `Vec` (nothing to render).\n/// - Query longer than text → one non-highlighted chunk.\n/// - `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` with any missing query character → that query\n///   contributes zero ranges (all-or-nothing).\npub fn highlight_chunks<'a>(props: &'a Props, locale: &Locale) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>>;\n```\n\n### 1.3 Build features\n\nThe agnostic-core lives in `ars-components` gated on `feature = \"i18n\"`, which enables `ars-i18n/icu4x`. The locale-aware case-mapping guarantee on `ignore_case = true` is non-negotiable, so the module is unavailable in builds that don't have ICU4X. Adapters using the browser-native `Intl` backend (`ars-i18n/web-intl`) cannot also use Highlight in the same build — the two ars-i18n backends are mutually exclusive.\n\n## 2. Anatomy\n\n```text\nHighlight\n└── Root                <span>                  data-ars-scope=\"highlight\" data-ars-part=\"root\" dir=\"auto\"\n    └── Chunk           <mark> | <span>   (×N) — parametric per `HighlightChunk`\n```\n\n| Part  | Element           | Key Attributes                                                                                                  |\n| ----- | ----------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| Root  | `<span>`          | `data-ars-scope=\"highlight\"`, `data-ars-part=\"root\"`, `dir=\"auto\"`                                              |\n| Chunk | `<mark>`/`<span>` | `data-ars-part=\"highlight-chunk\"`, `data-ars-highlighted=\"true\\|false\"` — emitted via `Api::chunk_attrs(bool)`  |\n\n**`Chunk` is a parametric anatomy slot.** It is intentionally absent from the `Part` enum because its attribute output depends on a runtime boolean (`highlighted`) that `ConnectApi::part_attrs` cannot carry. Adapters call `Api::chunk_attrs(chunk.highlighted)` per chunk. The same shape applies to other parametric anatomies in the catalog (per-row, per-item, per-tag slots).\n\nAdapters render each `HighlightChunk` as a `<mark>` (when `highlighted == true`) or `<span>` (when `highlighted == false`).\n\n## 3. Accessibility\n\n### 3.1 ARIA Roles, States, and Properties\n\n- `<mark>` is the semantic HTML element for highlighted text. Screen-reader announcement of `<mark>` content varies by browser/AT combination — VoiceOver and JAWS announce highlighted runs in many configurations; NVDA in its default profile does not. The agnostic core does not add ARIA attributes; adapters that need a guaranteed announcement (e.g., live-region tickers around the highlighted region) layer that on top.\n- For custom styling, adapters carry the `data-ars-part=\"highlight-chunk\"` and `data-ars-highlighted=\"true|false\"` attributes via `Api::chunk_attrs(highlighted)`.\n- **Adjacent / overlapping chunk consolidation is done in the agnostic core.** `highlight_chunks` merges overlapping and adjacent ranges before returning, so adapters never see back-to-back `<mark>` elements covering contiguous text and screen readers never get a stutter of \"highlighted, highlighted, highlighted\" for adjacent fuzzy hits. Adapters MAY still provide a summary announcement (e.g., \"N characters matched\") for high-cardinality fuzzy results, as a layered enhancement.\n\n### 3.2 Bidirectional Text\n\n`Api::root_attrs()` emits `dir=\"auto\"` on the root `<span>` so the browser picks the correct BiDi paragraph direction from the first strong character of the highlighted text — adapters do not need to add it themselves. Adapters MAY additionally apply `unicode-bidi: isolate` CSS to chunk elements when rendering English query terms inside Arabic or Hebrew text, to prevent chunk boundaries from re-ordering visually. The styling is a CSS concern outside the agnostic core's reach.\n\n## 4. Adapter Rendering\n\nAdapters spread `Api::root_attrs()` and `Api::chunk_attrs(highlighted)` rather than hardcoding the `data-ars-*` strings — keeping the attribute names typed and DRY against renames.\n\n```rust\n// Leptos example\nlet api = Api::new(props);\nlet chunks = api.chunks(&locale);\n\nview! {\n    <span ..api.root_attrs()>\n        {chunks.into_iter().map(|chunk| {\n            let attrs = api.chunk_attrs(chunk.highlighted);\n            if chunk.highlighted {\n                view! { <mark ..attrs>{chunk.text}</mark> }\n            } else {\n                view! { <span ..attrs>{chunk.text}</span> }\n            }\n        }).collect_view()}\n    </span>\n}\n```\n\n## 5. Library Parity\n\n> Compared against: Ark UI (`Highlight`).\n\n### 5.1 Props\n\n| Feature        | ars-ui                          | Ark UI                      | Notes                                         |\n| -------------- | ------------------------------- | --------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |\n| Query          | `query: Vec<String>`            | `query: string \\| string[]` | Both support multiple queries                 |\n| Text           | `text`                          | `text`                      | Both libraries                                |\n| Ignore case    | `ignore_case`                   | `ignoreCase`                | Both libraries                                |\n| Match strategy | `match_strategy: MatchStrategy` | `exactMatch`, `matchAll`    | ars-ui uses enum; Ark uses boolean flags      |\n| Locale         | `locale`                        | --                          | ars-ui addition for locale-aware case folding |\n\n**Gaps:** None.\n\n### 5.2 Anatomy\n\n| Part  | ars-ui              | Ark UI              | Notes                                        |\n| ----- | ------------------- | ------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |\n| Root  | `Root`              | (inline rendering)  | Both produce chunks for rendering            |\n| Chunk | `<mark>` / `<span>` | `<mark>` / `<span>` | Both libraries use mark for highlighted text |\n\n**Gaps:** None.\n\n### 5.3 Features\n\n| Feature                                 | ars-ui                            | Ark UI |\n| --------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- | ------ |\n| Multi-query                             | Yes                               | Yes    |\n| Case-insensitive                        | Yes                               | Yes    |\n| Fuzzy matching                          | Yes (`MatchStrategy::Fuzzy`)      | --     |\n| StartsWith matching                     | Yes (`MatchStrategy::StartsWith`) | --     |\n| Locale-aware folding                    | Yes                               | --     |\n| Adjacent/overlap merge in core          | Yes                               | --     |\n| `dir=\"auto\"` on root                    | Yes                               | --     |\n\n**Gaps:** None.\n\n### 5.4 Summary\n\n- **Overall:** Full parity — ars-ui is a superset.\n- **Divergences:** ars-ui uses `MatchStrategy` enum (Contains/StartsWith/Fuzzy) while Ark uses `exactMatch`/`matchAll` booleans. ars-ui adds locale-aware Unicode case folding, fuzzy matching, core-side adjacency merging, and a BiDi-safe root via `dir=\"auto\"`.\n- **Recommended additions:** None.\n"
}
```

`Highlight` does **not** derive `HasId` and has no `id` field — the rendered output is a sequence of chunks rather than a single addressable DOM element.

A fluent builder is provided per the workspace stateless-utility convention (see `foundation/10-component-spec-template.md` §4.1.2):

```rust
let props = Props::new()
    .query(["foo", "bar"])              // accepts any IntoIterator<Item = Into<String>>
    .text("foobar")
    .ignore_case(false)
    .match_strategy(MatchStrategy::Fuzzy);
```

### 1.2 Connect / API

```rust
/// One contiguous run of the original text, tagged as highlighted or not.
---

Adapters spread `Api::root_attrs()` and `Api::chunk_attrs(highlighted)` rather than hardcoding the `data-ars-*` strings — keeping the attribute names typed and DRY against renames.

```rust
// Leptos example
let api = Api::new(props);
let chunks = api.chunks(&locale);

view! {
    <span ..api.root_attrs()>
        {chunks.into_iter().map(|chunk| {
```

8	
9	# Highlight — Leptos Adapter
10	
11	## 1. Purpose and Adapter Scope
12	
13	This spec maps the core [`Highlight`](../../components/utility/highlight.md) utility to Leptos 0.8.x.
14	
15	## 2. Public Adapter API
16	
17	```rust,no_check
18	#[component] pub fn Highlight(...) -> impl IntoView
19	```
20	
21	## 3. Mapping to Core Component Contract
22	
23	- Props parity: full parity.
24	- Structure parity: root plus repeated chunk wrappers.
25	
26	## 4. Part Mapping
27	
28	| Core part / structure | Required? | Adapter rendering target | Ownership                               | Attr source                                                     | Notes                                             |
29	| --------------------- | --------- | ------------------------ | --------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
30	| `Root`                | required  | wrapper `<span>`         | adapter-owned                           | adapter-owned root attrs                                        | Core anatomy requires a root wrapper.             |
31	| highlight chunk       | repeated  | `<mark>` or `<span>`     | adapter-owned repeated structural nodes | adapter-owned structural attrs from `highlight_chunks()` output | Repeated structural node, not a core enum `Part`. |
32	
33	## 5. Attr Merge and Ownership Rules
34	
35	| Target node                      | Core attrs                                     | Adapter-owned attrs                                                                                  | Consumer attrs                                         | Merge order                                                                                                     | Ownership notes                                   |
36	| -------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
37	| root text container if present   | root attrs when the utility renders a wrapper  | chunk-boundary attrs plus optional mixed-direction `dir="auto"` repair                               | consumer wrapper attrs if exposed                      | core structural attrs win; documented directionality attrs and `class`/`style` merge additively                 | adapter-owned wrapper when present                |
38	| matched/unmatched chunk wrappers | chunk attrs derived from the core match result | adapter `data-*` part markers and optional `unicode-bidi: isolate` styles when bidi repair is needed | consumer decoration only if chunk rendering is exposed | match-state attrs and adapter-owned bidi repair win over conflicting decoration that would break text isolation | chunk wrappers are adapter-owned structural nodes |
39	
40	## 6. Composition / Context Contract
41	
42	No context contract.
43	
44	## 7. Prop Sync and Event Mapping
45	
46	Highlighting is derived from input text and query changes. If the adapter accepts reactive text or query props, updates are immediate and recomputed from props rather than through a long-lived interaction state machine.
47	
48	| Adapter prop | Mode       | Sync trigger            | Machine event / update path | Visible effect                      | Notes                  |
49	| ------------ | ---------- | ----------------------- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
50	| `text`       | controlled | prop change after mount | recompute highlight chunks  | updates rendered chunk wrappers     | effect-based recompute |
51	| `query`      | controlled | prop change after mount | recompute highlight chunks  | updates matched vs unmatched output | effect-based recompute |
52	
53	## 8. Registration and Cleanup Contract
54	
55	- No registration lifecycle exists beyond recomputing chunk wrappers on render.
56	- If chunk identity is keyed for animation, those keys are local render details and do not persist outside the component lifetime.
57	
58	## 9. Ref and Node Contract
59	
60	| Target part / node                         | Ref required?                                                                          | Ref owner                                             | Node availability                  | Composition rule                                                 | Notes                                                                              |
61	| ------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
62	| primary rendered node or provider boundary | no when the component is purely structural or provider-only; otherwise adapter-defined | adapter-owned unless part mapping says consumer-owned | always structural, handle optional | composed only when a consumer-owned node receives the core attrs | Use a live node handle only when the component's interaction contract requires it. |
63	
64	## 10. State Machine Boundary Rules
65	
66	- machine-owned state: all core interaction, accessibility, and controlled-state values defined by the component machine or derived API.
67	- adapter-local derived bookkeeping: minimal ephemeral data such as pointer modality, mount status, or observer handles when the core machine does not model them directly.
68	- forbidden local mirrors: do not fork controlled props, accessibility attrs, or machine-visible state into an unsynchronized local source of truth.
69	- allowed snapshot-read contexts: render-time derivation, event handlers, effects, and cleanup only when reading snapshots does not bypass required machine events.
70	
71	## 11. Callback Payload Contract
72	
73	| Callback                                                               | Payload source | Payload shape | Timing         | Cancelable? | Notes                                                                                                                 |
74	| ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------------- | -------------- | ----------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
75	| no public adapter-specific callback beyond normalized component events | none           | none          | not applicable | no          | When wrappers expose callbacks, they must preserve the normalized timing documented in `Prop Sync and Event Mapping`. |
76	
77	## 12. Failure and Degradation Rules
78	
79	| Condition                                                                  | Policy             | Notes                                                                             |
80	| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
81	| unsupported platform capability or missing browser-only API during SSR     | degrade gracefully | Render structural output and defer behavior until client-only APIs are available. |
82	| impossible prop combinations not explicitly supported by the core contract | fail fast          | Prefer an explicit contract violation over silently inventing behavior.           |
83	
84	## 13. Identity and Key Policy
85	
86	| Registered or repeated structure   | Identity source | Duplicates allowed? | DOM order must match registration order? | SSR/hydration stability                                                  | Notes                                                                                             |
87	| ---------------------------------- | --------------- | ------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
88	| none beyond the component instance | not applicable  | not applicable      | not applicable                           | stable root structure required where the component renders on the server | Use a component-instance identity only for cleanup bookkeeping when no repeated structure exists. |
89	
90	## 14. SSR and Client Boundary Rules
91	
92	- The server must render every structural node required by the part-mapping table unless the component is explicitly provider-only or client-gated.
93	- Client-only listeners, timers, measurements, and node-handle work must wait until hydration or mount.
94	- Any node that participates in hydration-sensitive semantics must preserve the same structural identity across server and client render paths.
95	
96	## 15. Performance Constraints
97	
98	- Attr maps derived from the machine should be memoized or read through adapter derivation helpers instead of rebuilt eagerly on every render.
99	- Listener, timer, and observer registration must be stable across rerenders and must not churn unless the governing configuration actually changes.
100	- Cleanup must release only the resources owned by the current component instance and must avoid repeated quadratic teardown work.
101	
102	## 16. Implementation Dependencies
103	
104	| Dependency                                  | Required?   | Dependency type | Why it must exist first                                                                                         | Notes                                                                 |
105	| ------------------------------------------- | ----------- | --------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
106	| none beyond the documented utility contract | recommended | conceptual      | The component may still rely on shared adapter patterns even when no specific prerequisite utility is required. | State `not applicable` explicitly if there is no stronger dependency. |
107	
108	## 17. Recommended Implementation Sequence
109	
110	1. Establish the machine/context boundary and confirm the documented part mapping.
111	2. Establish any required refs, provider context, or registration surfaces.
112	3. Derive attrs and render the documented structural nodes.
113	4. Wire prop sync and normalized event handling.
114	5. Add SSR/client-only guards, cleanup, and verify the documented test oracles.
115	
116	## 18. Anti-Patterns
117	
118	- Do not mirror machine-owned state in unsynchronized local adapter state.
119	- Do not bypass the documented attr merge order or replace required structural nodes with equivalent-looking wrappers.
120	- Do not re-register listeners, timers, observers, or registries on every render when the governing configuration has not changed.
121	
122	## 19. Consumer Expectations and Guarantees
123	
124	- Consumers may assume documented adapter-owned structural nodes and attrs remain the canonical implementation surface.
125	- Consumers may assume framework-specific divergence is called out explicitly rather than hidden in generic prose.
126	- Consumers must not assume unspecified fallback behavior, cleanup ordering, or helper ownership beyond what this adapter spec documents.
127	
128	## 20. Platform Support Matrix
129	
130	| Capability / behavior                     | Browser client | SSR          | Notes                                                                                         |
131	| ----------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
132	| documented structural and state semantics | full support   | full support | This utility does not have additional platform variance beyond its existing SSR/client rules. |
133	
134	## 21. Debug Diagnostics and Production Policy
135	
136	| Condition                                                            | Debug build behavior | Production behavior | Notes                                                                               |
137	| -------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
138	| no component-specific diagnostics beyond documented failure policies | not applicable       | not applicable      | Use the `Failure and Degradation Rules` section as the full runtime policy surface. |
139	
140	## 22. Shared Adapter Helper Notes
141	
142	| Helper concept    | Required?      | Responsibility                                                                          | Reused by      | Notes                                                         |
143	| ----------------- | -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
144	| attr merge helper | not applicable | No special helper beyond the documented attr derivation and merge contract is required. | not applicable | Use the normal machine attr derivation path for this utility. |
145	
146	## 23. Framework-Specific Behavior
147	
148	Leptos can render repeated chunks directly from an iterator. For mixed-direction content, the adapter should set `dir="auto"` on the root wrapper and may apply `unicode-bidi: isolate` on repeated `<mark>` and `<span>` chunk nodes when the highlighted text would otherwise bleed bidi ordering into surrounding content.
149	
150	## 24. Canonical Implementation Sketch
151	
152	```rust
153	#[component]
154	pub fn Highlight() -> impl IntoView {
155	    let props = highlight::Props::default();
156	    view! {
157	        <span data-ars-scope="highlight" data-ars-part="root">
158	            {highlight::highlight_chunks(&props).into_iter().map(|chunk| {
159	                if chunk.highlighted {
160	                    view! { <mark data-ars-part="highlight-chunk">{chunk.text}</mark> }.into_any()
161	                } else {
162	                    view! { <span data-ars-part="highlight-chunk">{chunk.text}</span> }.into_any()
163	                }
164	            }).collect_view()}
165	        </span>
166	    }
167	}
168	```
169	
170	## 25. Reference Implementation Skeleton
171	
172	No expanded skeleton beyond the canonical sketch is required for this utility.
173	
174	## 26. Adapter Invariants
175	
176	- Chunk wrappers must remain explicit structural nodes so matched and unmatched content do not lose part identity.
177	- Match consolidation rules must remain stable across rerenders so highlighting does not flicker or split unexpectedly.
178	- Locale-sensitive matching behavior must remain explicit wherever the core contract depends on it.
179	- Text isolation or bidi-sensitive output rules must be preserved wherever highlighted content can span mixed-direction text.
180	
181	## 27. Accessibility and SSR Notes
182	
183	Must preserve `<mark>` semantics and repeated chunk structure.
184	For fuzzy matching that would otherwise produce excessive tiny wrappers, the adapter should consolidate adjacent chunks when that reduces screen-reader verbosity without changing the user-visible matched ranges.
185	
186	## 28. Parity Summary and Intentional Deviations
187	
188	Parity summary: full core rendering parity.
189	
190	Intentional deviations: none.
191	
192	## 29. Test Scenarios
193	
194	- root mapping
195	- repeated chunk mapping
196	- highlighted vs non-highlighted chunk rendering
197	- mixed-direction highlighted content keeps expected root direction and chunk isolation
198	- fuzzy highlighting with many small chunks documents the chosen consolidation behavior
199	
200	## 30. Test Oracle Notes
201	
202	| Behavior                               | Preferred oracle type | Notes                                                                              |
203	| -------------------------------------- | --------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
204	| structural rendering and part presence | rendered structure    | Verify the documented part mapping rather than incidental wrapper details.         |
205	| accessibility and state attrs          | DOM attrs             | Assert the normalized attrs emitted by the adapter-owned node.                     |
206	| bidi handling                          | DOM attrs             | Assert root `dir="auto"` and any documented chunk-level isolation attrs or styles. |
207	| chunk consolidation policy             | rendered structure    | Verify repeated chunk output matches the documented fuzzy-match verbosity policy.  |
208	
209	## 31. Implementation Checklist
210	
211	- [ ] All documented parts and structural nodes are rendered with the correct ownership model.
212	- [ ] Attr merge precedence matches the documented contract.
213	- [ ] Prop sync and event normalization follow the documented machine paths.
214	- [ ] Cleanup releases every resource owned by the component instance.
215	- [ ] Mixed-direction root and chunk bidi handling is documented and verified when needed.
216	- [ ] Fuzzy-match chunk consolidation policy is explicit where many tiny wrappers would become noisy.
217	- [ ] SSR/client boundary behavior matches the documented structure and test oracles.
218	
```

Found three more areas worth fixing within scope, plus one nice-to-have:

1. **Adapter specs** (`spec/leptos-components/utility/highlight.md` and `spec/dioxus-components/utility/highlight.md`) — their canonical implementation sketches and attribute-merge tables still describe the old hardcoded-attr / no-locale / adapter-owns-`dir="auto"` model.
2. **Adapter feature wiring** — `ars-leptos/icu4x` and `ars-dioxus/icu4x` don't enable `ars-components/i18n`, so Highlight is invisible to adapter builds even when icu4x is on.
3. **Roundtrip invariant test** missing — *"concatenating all chunk texts equals the original text"* would catch a whole class of off-by-one regressions and isn't covered today.
4. **Proptest** — workspace has the infrastructure (`tests/proptest_state_machines/utility.rs`); Highlight has good invariants worth fuzzing.

Let me land them in order.

157	| attr merge helper | not applicable | No special helper beyond the documented attr derivation and merge contract is required. | not applicable | Use the normal machine attr derivation path for this utility. |
158	
159	## 23. Framework-Specific Behavior
160	
161	Dioxus can render repeated chunks directly from iterator output. For mixed-direction content, the adapter should set `dir="auto"` on the root wrapper and may apply `unicode-bidi: isolate` on repeated `<mark>` and `<span>` chunk nodes when the highlighted text would otherwise bleed bidi ordering into surrounding content.
162	
163	## 24. Canonical Implementation Sketch
164	
165	```rust
166	#[component]
167	pub fn Highlight() -> Element {
168	    let props = highlight::Props::default();
169	    rsx! {
170	        span { "data-ars-scope": "highlight", "data-ars-part": "root",
171	            {highlight::highlight_chunks(&props).into_iter().map(|chunk| {
172	                if chunk.highlighted {
173	                    rsx! { mark { "data-ars-part": "highlight-chunk", "{chunk.text}" } }
174	                } else {
175	                    rsx! { span { "data-ars-part": "highlight-chunk", "{chunk.text}" } }
176	                }
177	            })}
178	        }
179	    }
```

For mixed-direction content, the adapter should set `dir=\"auto\"` on the root wrapper and may apply `unicode-bidi: isolate` on repeated `<mark>` and `<span>` chunk nodes when the highlighted text would otherwise bleed bidi ordering into surrounding content.\n\n## 24. Canonical Implementation Sketch\n\n```rust\n#[component]\npub fn Highlight() -> impl IntoView {\n    let props = highlight::Props::default();\n    view! {\n        <span data-ars-scope=\"highlight\" data-ars-part=\"root\">\n            {highlight::highlight_chunks(&props).into_iter().map(|chunk| {\n                if chunk.highlighted {\n                    view! { <mark data-ars-part=\"highlight-chunk\">{chunk.text}</mark> }.into_any()\n                } else {\n                    view! { <span data-ars-part=\"highlight-chunk\">{chunk.text}</span> }.into_any()\n                }\n            }).collect_view()}\n        </span>\n    }\n}\n```",
  "new_string": "## 23. Framework-Specific Behavior\n\nLeptos renders repeated chunks directly from an iterator. The core sets `dir=\"auto\"` on the root wrapper via `Api::root_attrs()` — adapters do not need to add it. For mixed-direction content where chunk boundaries would re-order surrounding text visually, adapters MAY apply `unicode-bidi: isolate` CSS to chunk nodes (this is a styling concern outside the agnostic core's reach).\n\n## 24. Canonical Implementation Sketch\n\n```rust,no_check\n#[component]\npub fn Highlight() -> impl IntoView {\n    let props = highlight::Props::default();\n    let api = highlight::Api::new(props);\n    let locale = use_locale();\n\n    view! {\n        <span ..api.root_attrs()>\n            {api.chunks(&locale).into_iter().map(|chunk| {\n                let attrs = api.chunk_attrs(chunk.highlighted);\n                if chunk.highlighted {\n                    view! { <mark ..attrs>{chunk.text}</mark> }.into_any()\n                } else {\n                    view! { <span ..attrs>{chunk.text}</span> }.into_any()\n                }\n            }).collect_view()}\n        </span>\n    }\n}\n```"
}

Adapters MAY layer a summary announcement (e.g., live-region \"N characters matched\") for very-high-cardinality fuzzy results."

Canonical Implementation Sketch\n\n```rust\n#[component]\npub fn Highlight() -> Element {\n    let props = highlight::Props::default();\n    rsx! {\n        span { \"data-ars-scope\": \"highlight\", \"data-ars-part\": \"root\",\n            {highlight::highlight_chunks(&props).into_iter().map(|chunk| {\n                if chunk.highlighted {\n                    rsx! { mark { \"data-ars-part\": \"highlight-chunk\", \"{chunk.text}\" } }\n                } else {\n                    rsx! { span { \"data-ars-part\": \"highlight-chunk\", \"{chunk.text}\" } }\n                }\n            })}\n        }\n    }",

## 25. Reference Implementation Skeleton

No expanded skeleton beyond the canonical sketch is required for this utility.

## 26. Adapter Invariants

- Chunk wrappers must remain explicit structural nodes so matched and unmatched content do not lose part identity.
- Match consolidation rules must remain stable across rerenders so highlighting does not flicker or split unexpectedly.
- Locale-sensitive matching behavior must remain explicit wherever the core contract depends on it.
- Text isolation or bidi-sensitive output rules must be preserved wherever highlighted content can span mixed-direction text.
```

741	    #[test]
742	    fn unicode_case_folding_german_eszett() {
743	        // "SS" query under de locale should fold to "ss", which matches the
744	        // expansion of "ß". `to_lowercase("SS", de) == "ss"` and
745	        // `to_lowercase("ß", de) == "ß"`, so the byte-level match would
746	        // fail — but `to_lowercase("ß", de)` is NOT "ss", it's "ß" (lower
747	        // is already lower). So the correct test direction is: query "ss"
748	        // matches "Straße" (the "ß" doesn't expand under lower, but query
749	        // "SS" lowercased is "ss" which matches "ss" if the text has it).
750	        // We test the canonical case: query "ss" finds "ss" produced from
751	        // an uppercase "SS" via case-insensitive Contains.
752	        let props = Props::new().query(["ss"]).text("Straße ist STRASSE");
753	
754	        let chunks = highlight_chunks(&props, &german());

  "new_string": "    // ── Invariant tests ────────────────────────────────────────────\n\n    fn assert_chunks_concatenate_to_text(chunks: &[HighlightChunk<'_>], expected: &str) {\n        let concatenated: String = chunks.iter().map(|c| c.text).collect();\n        assert_eq!(\n            concatenated, expected,\n            \"chunks must reconstruct the original text\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    fn assert_no_two_adjacent_highlighted(chunks: &[HighlightChunk<'_>]) {\n        for window in chunks.windows(2) {\n            assert!(\n                !(window[0].highlighted && window[1].highlighted),\n                \"two adjacent highlighted chunks should have been merged: {window:?}\"\n            );\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn assert_no_empty_chunks(chunks: &[HighlightChunk<'_>]) {\n        for chunk in chunks {\n            assert!(\n                !chunk.text.is_empty(),\n                \"highlight_chunks must never emit an empty chunk: {chunk:?}\"\n            );\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn chunks_concatenate_to_original_text_across_strategies() {\n        // Roundtrip invariant: for any input, concatenating the chunk\n        // texts in order produces the original text verbatim. Exercised\n        // here across every strategy + ignore_case combination to catch\n        // off-by-one / boundary regressions silently producing wrong text.\n        let text = \"Foo bar BAZ ßaz İstanbul fooo\";\n        let queries: &[&[&str]] = &[\n            &[\"foo\"],\n            &[\"BAR\"],\n            &[\"foo\", \"BAZ\"],\n            &[\"ßaz\"],\n            &[\"i\"],\n            &[\"z\"],\n            &[],\n            &[\"\"],\n            &[\"\", \"foo\"],\n        ];\n\n        let strategies = [\n            MatchStrategy::Contains,\n            MatchStrategy::StartsWith,\n            MatchStrategy::Fuzzy,\n        ];\n\n        for ignore_case in [true, false] {\n            for query in queries {\n                for strategy in strategies {\n                    let props = Props::new()\n                        .query(query.iter().copied())\n                        .text(text)\n                        .ignore_case(ignore_case)\n                        .match_strategy(strategy);\n\n                    let chunks = highlight_chunks(&props, &en_us());\n\n                    assert_chunks_concatenate_to_text(&chunks, text);\n                    assert_no_two_adjacent_highlighted(&chunks);\n                    assert_no_empty_chunks(&chunks);\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn ignore_case_true_matches_case_insensitive() {"
}

---
//! Shared configuration for the property-based state-machine tests.
//!
//! Centralises two concerns that every `proptest!` block in this harness
//! cares about:
//!
//! 1. **Case count.** Reads the `PROPTEST_CASES` environment variable so
//!    the nightly `extended-proptest` job (10,000 cases) and local runs
//!    (1,000 cases by default) share one knob.
//! 2. **Failure persistence.** Redirects the `*.proptest-regressions`
//!    seed files to a single per-crate `proptest-regressions/`
//!    directory at the crate root instead of dropping them next to test
//!    sources. Default proptest behaviour clutters the source tree with
//!    a sibling file per test module; this single-file layout is the
//!    same content but lives next to `Cargo.toml` where bookkeeping
//!    files belong. The file is committed (per proptest convention) so
//!    every developer's runs benefit from previously-shrunk failures.
//!
//! Use as `#![proptest_config(super::common::proptest_config())]` inside
//! every `proptest!` block in this harness.

use proptest::test_runner::{Config, FileFailurePersistence};

/// Builds a `ProptestConfig` with the workspace-standard case count and
/// failure-persistence layout.
///
