    #[test]
    #[ignore = "proptest — nightly extended-proptest job"]
    fn proptest_error_boundary_aria_primitives_are_count_invariant(
        a in 0usize..=10_000,
        b in 0usize..=10_000,
    ) {
        let attrs_a = error_boundary::Api::new(a).root_attrs();
        let attrs_b = error_boundary::Api::new(b).root_attrs();

        for attr in [
            HtmlAttr::Role,
            HtmlAttr::Aria(ars_core::AriaAttr::Live),
            HtmlAttr::Aria(ars_core::AriaAttr::Atomic),
            HtmlAttr::Data("ars-scope"),
            HtmlAttr::Data("ars-part"),
            HtmlAttr::Data("ars-error"),
        ] {
            prop_assert_eq!(
                attrs_a.get(&attr),
                attrs_b.get(&attr),
                "attr {:?} should be count-invariant", attr
            );
        }
    }
}

\"[a-zA-Z0-9 _\\u{00DF}\\u{0130}\\u{00E9}]{0,32}\".prop_map(String::from)\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"i18n\")]\nfn arb_highlight_query() -> impl Strategy<Value = Vec<String>> {\n    prop::collection::vec(\"[a-zA-Z0-9 \\u{00DF}\\u{0130}]{0,8}\".prop_map(String::from), 0..4)\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"i18n\")]\nfn arb_highlight_props() -> impl Strategy<Value = highlight::Props> {\n    (\n        arb_highlight_query(),\n        arb_highlight_text(),\n        any::<bool>(),\n        arb_match_strategy(),\n    )\n        .prop_map(|(query, text, ignore_case, match_strategy)| {\n Just(Locale::parse(\"tr\").expect(\"tr must parse\")),\n        Just(Locale::parse(\"de\").expect(\"de must parse\")),\n        Just(Locale::parse(\"el\").expect(\"el must parse\")),\n        Just(Locale::parse(\"ar\").expect(\"ar must parse\")),\n    ]\n}\n\n#[cfg(feature = \"i18n\")]\nproptest! {\n    #![proptest_config(super::common::proptest_config())]\n\n    /// For any combination of props and locale, `highlight_chunks` must:\n    ///\n    /// 1. **Never panic** (covered by the test reaching its end).\n    /// 2. **Roundtrip the text** — concatenating all chunk slices in order\n    ///    yields the original `props.text` byte-for-byte. Catches any\n    ///    range / index / byte-map regression silently dropping or\n    ///    duplicating text.\n    /// 3. **Never emit two adjacent highlighted chunks** — the agnostic\n    ///    core's adjacency-merge contract (spec §3.1) must hold for every\n    ///    strategy and every query combination.\n    /// 4. **Never emit empty chunks** — zero-length segments are skipped\n    ///    by `build_chunks`; this guards against silent regressions there.\n    /// 5. **Empty query (or all-empty queries)** must produce exactly one\n    ///    non-highlighted chunk wrapping the full text, when the text is\n    ///    non-empty. Empty text yields an empty `Vec`.\n    #[test]\n    #[ignore = \"proptest — nightly extended-proptest job\"]\n    fn proptest_highlight_chunks_invariants(\n        props in arb_highlight_props(),\n        locale in arb_highlight_locale(),\n    ) {\n        let chunks = highlight::highlight_chunks(&props, &locale);\n\n        // (2) roundtrip\n        let concatenated: String = chunks.iter().map(|c| c.text).collect();\n        prop_assert_eq!(\n            concatenated.as_str(),\n            props.text.as_str(),\n            \"chunks must reconstruct the original text\"\n        );\n\n        // (3) no two adjacent highlighted chunks\n        for window in chunks.windows(2) {\n            prop_assert!(\n                !(window[0].highlighted && window[1].highlighted),\n                \"adjacency-merge regression: {:?}\", window\n            );\n        }\n\n        // (4) no empty chunks\n        for chunk in &chunks {\n            prop_assert!(\n                !chunk.text.is_empty(),\n                \"empty chunk emitted: {:?}\", chunk\n            );\n        }\n\n        // (5) empty-query / empty-text special cases\n        let all_queries_empty = props.query.iter().all(String::is_empty);\n        if props.text.is_empty() {\n            prop_assert!(chunks.is_empty(), \"empty text should yield empty Vec\");\n        } else if props.query.is_empty() || all_queries_empty {\n            prop_assert_eq!(chunks.len(), 1, \"empty query → exactly one chunk\");\n            prop_assert!(!chunks[0].highlighted, \"empty-query chunk must not be highlighted\");\n            prop_assert_eq!(chunks[0].text, props.text.as_str());\n        }\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

## Additional improvements landed

| Improvement | Where |
|---|---|
| **Leptos adapter spec aligned** | `spec/leptos-components/utility/highlight.md` — §5 attr-merge table now lists `Api::root_attrs()` / `Api::chunk_attrs(bool)` and identifies `dir="auto"` as core-owned; §23 says core owns BiDi; §24 canonical sketch uses `Api`, `Api::root_attrs()` / `chunk_attrs()` spread, and passes `&locale`; §27 says core does the adjacency consolidation, adapter MAY layer summary announcements. |
| **Dioxus adapter spec aligned** | `spec/dioxus-components/utility/highlight.md` — same edits as Leptos, with Dioxus 0.7 `..attrs` spread syntax in the sketch. |
| **Adapter feature wiring** | `ars-leptos/Cargo.toml` and `ars-dioxus/Cargo.toml` — `icu4x` feature now also enables `ars-components/i18n`, so any adapter build with `icu4x` on automatically pulls in Highlight. Verified by `cargo build -p ars-leptos --features icu4x` and `cargo build -p ars-dioxus --features icu4x,web`. |
| **Roundtrip + invariant unit test** | `crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs` — `chunks_concatenate_to_original_text_across_strategies` exercises the full strategy × ignore_case × query matrix and asserts (a) concatenation equals input text, (b) no two adjacent highlighted chunks, (c) no empty chunks. Catches an entire class of byte-boundary / merge regressions in one place. |
| **Property test** | `crates/ars-components/tests/proptest_state_machines/utility.rs` — `proptest_highlight_chunks_invariants` fuzzes (props × locale) and asserts (a) never panics, (b) chunks roundtrip the text, (c) no adjacent highlighted, (d) no empty chunks, (e) empty-query / empty-text edge cases. Strategies cover Latin + multi-byte (`ß`, `İ`, `é`) text against en-US, tr, de, el, ar locales. 1000 cases pass in ~0.19s; the nightly `extended-proptest` job runs 10,000. |

## Verification

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `cargo test -p ars-components --features i18n --all-targets` | 1500 lib + 3 conformance pass (+1 from invariant test, +1 from proptest [ignored unless --include-ignored]) |
| `cargo test -p ars-components --lib` (no `i18n`) | 1463 pass; Highlight module gated out cleanly |
| `cargo test … proptest_highlight -- --include-ignored` | passes (1000 cases ≈ 0.19s) |
| `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --exclude ars-i18n -- -D warnings` | clean |
| `cargo build -p ars-leptos --features icu4x` | clean (Highlight now reachable in Leptos with icu4x) |
| `cargo build -p ars-dioxus --features icu4x,web` | clean (Highlight now reachable in Dioxus with icu4x) |
| `cargo xtask spec validate` | 340 files, all checks passed |
| `cargo test -p xtask --test spec_corpus_compile_snippets` | passes (no_check tags on illustrative snippets) |
| `cargo insta test --workspace … --unreferenced=reject` | no orphan snapshots |

## What I considered and rejected

| Considered | Why not |
|---|---|
| **Promote "parametric anatomy slot" to `foundation/10`** | Genuinely a reusable concept (tabs triggers, tags-input chips, toaster toasts all have this shape), but the right move is a cross-cutting foundation refactor with examples drawn from those components too — too broad for this task. Documenting it inline in Highlight's spec for now. |
| **Replace per-char `to_lowercase` allocation in `build_normalised` with a streaming/chunked approach** | The per-char churn is real but `CaseMapper::new()` returns a `Copy` borrowed type, so the only allocations are per-char output `String`s. Optimising this requires either (a) a streaming `CaseMapper` API (doesn't exist in icu_casemap 2.x) or (b) lowering the whole text once and re-deriving the byte-map by parallel iteration on char boundaries — which is fragile because case mappings can split a single input char into multiple output chars (and vice versa for titlecasing). Algorithmic correctness > micro-perf here. |
| **Broaden `Props::query` builder from `Into<String>` to `AsRef<str>`** | Would accept `&[&str]` (which currently doesn't compile) in addition to `[&str; N]` / `Vec<&str>` / `Vec<String>`. Minor ergonomic gain at the cost of an extra `.to_owned()` per element. All in-tree call sites use array-literal form, which already works. Skipped. |
| **Update `foundation/02-component-catalog.md` status** | Already shows `Y` (implemented) for Highlight — no edit needed. |

Now everything is genuinely landed and verified. Awaiting your sign-off; once approved I'll run `cargo xci`, commit, push, open the PR with `Closes #204`, and apply `snapshot-reviewed`.

1	//! Convenience re-exports for end users of the Leptos adapter.
2	//!
3	//! A single `use ars_leptos::prelude::*;` gives application authors access to
4	//! the components, user-facing traits, and configuration types they need —
5	//! without hunting through individual `ars_*` crates.
6	//!
7	//! # What belongs in the prelude
8	//!
9	//! Only items that **end users** interact with directly:
10	//!
11	//! 1. **Component modules** — as components land (e.g., `button`, `dialog`,
12	//!    `select`), their public module paths are re-exported here so users can
13	//!    write `button::Props`, `dialog::Machine`, etc.
14	//!
15	//! 2. **User-facing traits** — traits that end users call on component outputs
16	//!    (e.g., `Translate` from `ars-i18n` for localised strings). Re-exporting
17	//!    the trait avoids forcing users to add a direct dependency on the
18	//!    subsystem crate just to call a method.
19	//!
20	//! 3. **Configuration types** — types that appear in component props or that
21	//!    users pass to configure behaviour (e.g., `Locale`, `Direction`,
22	//!    `Orientation`, `Selection`).
23	//!
24	//! # What does NOT belong in the prelude
25	//!
26	//! - **Core engine internals** — `Machine`, `Service`, `ConnectApi`,
27	//!   `ComponentPart`, `AttrMap`, `Bindable`, `TransitionPlan`, `PendingEffect`.
28	//!   These are implementation details used by component authors *inside* the
29	//!   adapter crate, not by end users consuming components.
30	//! - **Accessibility primitives** — `AriaRole`, `AriaAttribute`, `ComponentIds`.
31	//!   Wired internally by each component; end users never construct these.
32	//! - **Interaction primitives** — `merge_attrs`, `PressState`, `FocusState`.
33	//!   Internal to component implementations.
34	//! - **Adapter hooks** — `use_machine`, `UseMachineReturn`, `EphemeralRef`.
35	//!   Used by component modules inside the adapter crate, not by end users
36	//!   who consume the ready-made components. (Still publicly accessible via
37	//!   `ars_leptos::use_machine` for advanced users building custom machines.)
38	//! - **Framework re-exports** — `leptos::prelude::*` should be imported
39	//!   separately; we do not re-export Leptos types to avoid version coupling.
40	//!
41	//! # Growth policy
42	//!
43	//! When adding a new item, ask: "Does an end user writing `<Button>` or
44	//! `<Dialog>` in their app need this?" If yes, add it. If only component
45	//! implementors inside this crate need it, keep it as a regular import.
46	
47	// -- User-facing traits --
48	pub use ars_collections::{Key, TabKey};
49	pub use ars_core::{I18nRegistries, MessageFn, MessagesRegistry, SafeUrl, UnsafeUrlError};
50	pub use ars_i18n::{Direction, IntlBackend, Locale, Orientation, ResolvedDirection, Translate};
51	
52	// -- Component modules --
53	//
54	// Consumers reach component types via the module qualifier
55	// (e.g. `dismissable::Props`, `dismissable::Region`, `dismissable::Handle`,
56	// `dismissable::DismissReason`, `dismissable::use_dismissable`); never via
57	// flattened aliases such as `DismissableProps` or `DismissableRegion`.
58	//
59	// The adapter `dismissable` module re-exports the agnostic
60	// `ars_components::utility::dismissable::*` surface, so this single
61	// re-export covers both the framework-agnostic types (`Props`, `Messages`,
62	// `DismissReason`, …) and the Leptos-side wrappers (`Handle`, `Region`,
63	// `RegionProps`, `use_dismissable`).
64	pub use crate::as_child;
65	pub use crate::navigation::{self, tabs};
66	// The `error_boundary` adapter module exposes the `ArsErrorBoundary`
67	// wrapper component spec'd at
68	// `spec/foundation/08-adapter-leptos.md` §17. End users reach it as
69	// `error_boundary::ArsErrorBoundary` after `use ars_leptos::prelude::*;`.
70	pub use crate::utility::{self, button, dismissable, error_boundary, separator, visually_hidden};
71	// -- User-facing helpers --
72	pub use crate::{Translatable, t, use_number_formatter};
73

> AGENT

Confirmed — there **is** a remaining issue worth flagging. The German eszett contract is **not actually satisfied** by the current implementation.

## Remaining finding — German eszett semantic gap (high)

I probed the impl directly. All three directions fail to match:

| Query | Text | Should match per "ß↔ss equivalence"? | Current behavior |
|---|---|---|---|
| `"SS"` | `"Straße"` | yes (uppercase-eszett = `SS`) | **no match** |
| `"ss"` | `"Straße"` | yes (canonical fold equivalence) | **no match** |
| `"ß"` | `"STRASSE"` | yes (reverse direction) | **no match** |

The reason is that `ars_i18n::to_lowercase` (which wraps `CaseMapper::lowercase_to_string`) **preserves** `ß` — it doesn't expand to `ss`:

- `to_lowercase("ß", de) → "ß"`
- `to_lowercase("SS", de) → "ss"`
- `to_lowercase("Straße", de) → "straße"`

So query `"ss"` searches for `"ss"` in `"straße"` and finds nothing. The current `unicode_case_folding_german_eszett` test passes only because it embeds `"SS"` inside `"STRASSE"` (uppercase-only word) where lowercase trivially produces `"ss"`. The actual contract — *"`ß` and `ss` should match each other"* — is silently violated.

The only way to honor this in ICU4X 2.x is **case folding** (`CaseMapper::fold_string`), which is exactly what the spec wording originally referenced before I had it changed:

- `fold_string("ß") → "ss"`
- `fold_string("SS") → "ss"`

When I asked you to pick a case-folding approach earlier, I claimed *"identical behaviour to fold on the named tests"* — that claim was wrong for the eszett case. Apologies.

### Recommended path

Switch to a fold-based helper. Two ways:

1. **Add a thin `fold_string` wrapper to `ars-i18n::case`** (matches my earlier "alternative" option). ~10 LOC + a few tests in `ars-i18n`, then change `locale_aware_lowercase` in `highlight.rs` to call it. Honours `ß ↔ ss`, Greek final sigma collapse, Turkic dotless-I, all in one shot.
2. **Drop the `ars-i18n` indirection** and use `icu::casemap::CaseMapper::fold_string` directly in `highlight.rs` (matches `ars-collections::typeahead.rs`'s precedent of importing `icu` directly). Adds `icu` as a direct dep of `ars-components` behind the existing `i18n` feature.

Either way, the eszett test should be rewritten to assert the actual equivalence (`"ss"` matches `"ß"` in `"Straße"`), and similarly for `"SS"`.

---

## Smaller remaining items

### Mutation testing scout (medium)
The workspace runs `cargo-mutants` nightly. Running it now on `highlight.rs` (estimated ~3–5 min based on file size relative to the popover example) would surface any surviving mutations before nightly CI does. The "missed" entries are real test gaps — exactly the class of bug that line-coverage misses but the workspace cares about.

### Foundation `10-component-spec-template.md` — "parametric anatomy slot" concept (low)
A two-sentence addition under §4.2 explaining that not every anatomy row maps to a `Part` enum variant — parts whose attributes depend on runtime data (per-row, per-item, per-chunk) live as methods on `Api` instead. Documents the convention I introduced inline in Highlight's spec, and gets ahead of the same pattern in tables / tags-input / toaster.

### Turkish "I" dilemma — adapter-facing doc, not a code change (low)
Under tr locale with `to_lowercase`: query `"Ist"` lowercases to `"ıst"` (dotless), text `"İstanbul"` lowercases to `"istanbul"` (dotted) — they don't match. Switching to `fold_turkic` would fix this too. If we switch to fold (option above), this gets fixed for free. If we don't, the spec should call this out so adapters know to either lower-case the query themselves before passing it in or accept the limitation.

---

## My recommendation

Land the **fold switch** now. It:

1. Closes the actual eszett gap (highest impact).
2. Fixes the Turkic-uppercase corollary at the same time.
3. Matches the original spec wording before I rewrote it ("Unicode case folding via ICU4X `CaseMapper::fold_turkic()` / `CaseMapper::fold()`").
4. Is what TR21 explicitly says to do for case-insensitive matching — `to_lowercase` was always a near-miss for this use case.

I'd also run cargo-mutants as a second pass to catch any remaining test gaps.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/crates/ars-i18n/src/case.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Locale-aware lowercase transformation.\n///\n/// Delegates to ICU4X [`CaseMapper`] so locale-specific mappings such as\n/// Turkish dotless i and Greek final sigma are preserved. Under the wasm\n/// `web-intl` backend, this delegates to browser\n/// `String.prototype.toLocaleLowerCase()`. On non-wasm builds with `web-intl`,\n/// it falls back to Unicode case conversion so the public API remains\n/// available in feature-matrix builds.\n#[must_use]\n#[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\npub fn to_lowercase(text: &str, locale: &Locale) -> String {\n    CaseMapper::new()\n        .lowercase_to_string(text, locale.language_identifier())\n        .into_owned()\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Locale-aware lowercase transformation.\n///\n/// Delegates to ICU4X [`CaseMapper`] so locale-specific mappings such as\n/// Turkish dotless i and Greek final sigma are preserved. Under the wasm\n/// `web-intl` backend, this delegates to browser\n/// `String.prototype.toLocaleLowerCase()`. On non-wasm builds with `web-intl`,\n/// it falls back to Unicode case conversion so the public API remains\n/// available in feature-matrix builds.\n#[must_use]\n#[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\npub fn to_lowercase(text: &str, locale: &Locale) -> String {\n    CaseMapper::new()\n        .lowercase_to_string(text, locale.language_identifier())\n        .into_owned()\n}\n\n/// Unicode case folding for case-insensitive comparison.\n///\n/// Returns the canonical case-fold form per Unicode Technical Report 21 —\n/// the right primitive for case-insensitive **matching** (as opposed to\n/// case **transformation**, which is what [`to_lowercase`] / [`to_uppercase`]\n/// do). The key differences for matching:\n///\n/// - German eszett: `fold(\"ß\") == fold(\"SS\") == \"ss\"`, so `\"ß\"` and `\"ss\"`\n///   match each other (whereas `to_lowercase(\"ß\") == \"ß\"`).\n/// - Greek final sigma collapses into the medial form, so a query against\n///   text containing `\"Ος\"` matches `\"ΟΣ\"` and vice versa.\n/// - Turkic dotted/dotless I: when `locale` is a Turkic language (`tr` /\n///   `az`), the implementation switches to ICU4X\n///   `CaseMapper::fold_turkic_string` so `İ ↔ i` and `I ↔ ı` per the\n///   Turkic case-folding convention. Non-Turkic locales use the default\n///   Unicode fold.\n///\n/// Available only under the `icu4x` feature; the `web-intl` backend has\n/// no equivalent (browser `Intl` exposes only locale-aware lowercase /\n/// uppercase) so callers requiring case-fold-based matching must use the\n/// ICU4X backend.\n#[must_use]\n#[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\npub fn case_fold(text: &str, locale: &Locale) -> String {\n    let mapper = CaseMapper::new();\n    if is_turkic_locale(locale) {\n        mapper.fold_turkic_string(text).into_owned()\n    } else {\n        mapper.fold_string(text).into_owned()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Returns `true` for locales that use Turkic case-folding rules — Turkish\n/// (`tr`) and Azerbaijani (`az`).\n///\n/// These are the language tags ICU treats as Turkic for case mapping per\n/// CLDR's `special-casing` data, which is the same set that\n/// `CaseMapper::lowercase_to_string` already switches behaviour on. Other\n/// Turkic family languages (Kazakh `kk`, Tatar `tt`, Uzbek `uz`) follow\n/// regular Unicode case rules in CLDR.\n#[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\nfn is_turkic_locale(locale: &Locale) -> bool {\n    matches!(locale.language(), \"tr\" | \"az\")\n}"
}

  "old_string": "    #[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\n    #[test]\n    fn greek_lowercase_applies_final_sigma_rules() {\n        let locale = Locale::parse(\"el\").expect(\"locale should parse\");\n\n        assert_eq!(to_lowercase(\"ΟΣ\", &locale), \"ος\");\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\n    #[test]\n    fn greek_lowercase_applies_final_sigma_rules() {\n        let locale = Locale::parse(\"el\").expect(\"locale should parse\");\n\n        assert_eq!(to_lowercase(\"ΟΣ\", &locale), \"ος\");\n    }\n\n    // ── case_fold (Unicode case folding for case-insensitive match) ──\n\n    #[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\n    #[test]\n    fn fold_expands_eszett_to_ss() {\n        // Per Unicode TR21, `case_fold(\"ß\") == \"ss\"` and `case_fold(\"SS\")\n        // == \"ss\"`, so the two are equivalent for case-insensitive\n        // matching. (Lowercase preserves `ß`; only the fold form expands.)\n        let de = Locale::parse(\"de\").expect(\"locale should parse\");\n\n        assert_eq!(super::case_fold(\"ß\", &de), \"ss\");\n        assert_eq!(super::case_fold(\"SS\", &de), \"ss\");\n        assert_eq!(super::case_fold(\"Straße\", &de), \"strasse\");\n    }\n\n    #[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\n    #[test]\n    fn fold_collapses_greek_final_sigma_into_medial_form() {\n        // Final sigma `ς` and medial sigma `σ` both fold to `σ`, and\n        // capital `Σ` folds to the same. So queries against text mixing\n        // the three forms still match under case_fold.\n        let el = Locale::parse(\"el\").expect(\"locale should parse\");\n\n        assert_eq!(super::case_fold(\"ΟΣ\", &el), super::case_fold(\"Ος\", &el));\n        assert_eq!(super::case_fold(\"σ\", &el), super::case_fold(\"ς\", &el));\n    }\n\n    #[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\n    #[test]\n    fn fold_turkic_locale_uses_turkic_fold() {\n        // Under `tr` / `az`, `İ ↔ i` and `I ↔ ı`. Under non-Turkic\n        // locales the standard Unicode fold applies (`İ → i̇`).\n        let tr = Locale::parse(\"tr\").expect(\"locale should parse\");\n        let en = Locale::parse(\"en-US\").expect(\"locale should parse\");\n\n        // Turkic: dotted/dotless I pairings collapse.\n        assert_eq!(super::case_fold(\"İ\", &tr), super::case_fold(\"i\", &tr));\n        assert_eq!(super::case_fold(\"I\", &tr), super::case_fold(\"ı\", &tr));\n\n        // Non-Turkic: `İ` does NOT fold to plain `i`.\n        assert_ne!(super::case_fold(\"İ\", &en), \"i\");\n    }\n\n    #[cfg(feature = \"icu4x\")]\n    #[test]\n    fn fold_is_idempotent() {\n        // Folding an already-folded string is a no-op (the fold form is\n        // its own canonical representative).\n        for input in [\"hello\", \"Straße\", \"İstanbul\", \"ΟΣ\"] {\n            let de = Locale::parse(\"de\").expect(\"locale should parse\");\n            let folded = super::case_fold(input, &de);\n            assert_eq!(super::case_fold(&folded, &de), folded);\n        }\n    }"
}

The fold form — not lowercase — is the right primitive for\n/// matching: it expands `ß → ss`, collapses Greek final sigma into the\n/// medial form, and (under Turkic) folds `İ ↔ i` / `I ↔ ı`. This is what\n/// the spec's `ignore_case = true` contract requires.\nfn case_fold(text: &str, locale: &Locale) -> String {\n    ars_i18n::case_fold(text, locale)\n}"
}

  "old_string": "/// Splits the text into alternating highlighted / non-highlighted segments.\n///\n/// When `ignore_case` is `true`, matching uses locale-aware Unicode case\n/// mapping via `ars_i18n::to_lowercase` (which wraps ICU4X\n/// `CaseMapper::lowercase_to_string` with the locale's language identifier).\n/// This ensures correct matching for Turkish dotted/dotless I (`İ`/`i` vs\n/// `I`/`ı`), German eszett (`ß`/`SS`), Greek final sigma, Lithuanian\n/// dotted I, and other locale-tailored case mappings supplied by CLDR.",
  "new_string": "/// Splits the text into alternating highlighted / non-highlighted segments.\n///\n/// When `ignore_case` is `true`, matching uses Unicode **case folding**\n/// (per Unicode Technical Report 21 — the canonical primitive for\n/// case-insensitive matching, as opposed to case **transformation** which\n/// is what `to_lowercase` / `to_uppercase` do) via `ars_i18n::case_fold`.\n/// This wraps ICU4X `CaseMapper::fold_string` (or `fold_turkic_string`\n/// under Turkic locales) and delivers true case equivalence:\n///\n/// - German eszett: `\"ß\"`, `\"ss\"`, and `\"SS\"` all match each other\n///   (the fold expands `ß → ss`).\n/// - Greek: final-sigma `ς`, medial `σ`, and capital `Σ` all collapse to\n///   the same fold form.\n/// - Turkic (`tr` / `az`): `İ ↔ i` and `I ↔ ı` per the Turkic fold; other\n///   locales use the standard Unicode fold (`İ → i\\u{307}`).\n/// - Lithuanian, Armenian, and other CLDR-tailored mappings supplied by\n///   `CaseMapper`."
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/spec/foundation/10-component-spec-template.md",
  "old_string": "Parts must be marked as required (component broken without it) or optional (enhances UX, degrades gracefully).\n\n### 4.3 Accessibility",
  "new_string": "Parts must be marked as required (component broken without it) or optional (enhances UX, degrades gracefully).\n\n#### Static vs parametric anatomy slots\n\nMost parts are **static**: exactly one DOM element per component instance per part, and the part appears as a variant in the component's `Part` enum so `ConnectApi::part_attrs(Part) -> AttrMap` can address it. This is the default and applies to the majority of the catalog.\n\nA minority of components have **parametric** anatomy slots — anatomy positions that emit zero-to-N elements per component instance, where the emitted attributes depend on runtime data the `Part` enum cannot carry (a row key, an item value, a `highlighted` boolean). Examples: a per-chunk wrapper in `Highlight`, a per-row cell in `Table`, a per-tag chip in `TagsInput`. For these:\n\n- Keep the row in the Anatomy table and the ASCII diagram so the rendered shape is documented end-to-end.\n- Do **not** add a `Part` enum variant. `ConnectApi::part_attrs` takes a `Part` by value with no payload — it cannot express runtime parameters cleanly.\n- Expose the attributes through an `Api` method that takes the runtime parameter explicitly (e.g., `Api::chunk_attrs(highlighted: bool) -> AttrMap`, `Api::row_attrs(&row_key) -> AttrMap`). Document the method name in the Anatomy parts-table's \"Key Attributes\" column.\n- Note the slot's parametric nature in the spec text once, ideally right under the table.\n\nThe convention keeps the static-`Part` happy path simple while giving parametric slots a typed, discoverable API.\n\n### 4.3 Accessibility"
}
```

172	  # Tabs `step_focus` checked-counter mutations and the Blur
173	  # short-circuit are equivalent in every reachable state. Regex is

179	  # - `> → ==` and `> → >=` on `checked > total` bail on the same
180	  #   iteration in every reachable input (loop advances by one per
181	  #   iteration, `total` is the upper bound on distinct positions).
182	  # - `&& → ||` in the Blur early-return only diverges when
183	  #   `state == Focused && focused_tab.is_none()` — unreachable
184	  #   because every Focused-producing transition sets focused_tab to
185	  #   Some, and Blur clears both atomically.

id: toolu_0133We2jFLusFTgMNRS6A7M5
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/.cargo/mutants.toml",
  "old_string": "  # Tabs `step_focus` checked-counter mutations and the Blur",
  "new_string": "  # Highlight component: three equivalent mutations in the range\n  # construction / merge / chunk-emission pipeline. All three are\n  # defensive guards against zero-length original-text ranges produced\n  # when a case-fold expansion (e.g., `ß → ss`) is partially matched.\n  # No code path in the pipeline can produce a zero-length range that\n  # would survive the *next* filter, so weakening any single guard\n  # produces equivalent end-to-end behaviour (verified by output diff\n  # against the test corpus).\n  #\n  # - `< → <=` in push_contains_matches: would push (a, a) ranges; those\n  #   then get filtered by build_chunks's `end > start` check before\n  #   emitting a chunk.\n  # - `> → >=` in merge_ranges: when `end == last.1`, the body becomes\n  #   `last.1 = last.1`, a no-op.\n  # - `> → >=` in build_chunks: would push zero-length highlighted\n  #   chunks, but no upstream pipeline stage produces zero-length ranges\n  #   (push_contains_matches filters them; push_starts_with_match\n  #   requires `orig_end > 0`; push_fuzzy_matches always uses\n  #   `len_utf8() >= 1`).\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace < with <= in push_contains_matches',\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace > with >= in merge_ranges',\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace > with >= in build_chunks',\n\n  # Tabs `step_focus` checked-counter mutations and the Blur"
}

> AGENT

## Round-three improvements landed

| Improvement | Where | Impact |
|---|---|---|
| **Switched matching from `to_lowercase` to TR21 case fold** | New `ars_i18n::case_fold(text, locale)` helper; `highlight.rs` calls it via `case_fold(...)` instead of `locale_aware_lowercase(...)` | Closes the German eszett gap: `"ss"`, `"SS"`, and `"ß"` now genuinely match each other in all directions. Also closes Greek final-sigma (σ/ς/Σ collapse) and Turkic-uppercase-query (`Ist` ↔ `İstanbul`) corollaries in one shot. |
| **Eszett test rewritten with three real directions** | `unicode_case_folding_german_eszett` | Tests now assert (a) query `"ss"` finds `"ß"` AND `"SS"`; (b) query `"SS"` finds `"ß"`; (c) query `"ß"` finds `"SS"`. Previously the test only asserted the trivial uppercase-to-lowercase direction and silently passed despite missing the contract. |
| **Foundation/10 `§4.2 Static vs parametric anatomy slots`** | `spec/foundation/10-component-spec-template.md` | Documents the convention I introduced inline for Highlight so future tables / tags-input / toaster work follows the same pattern (Anatomy row stays; no `Part` enum variant; expose attrs via parameterised `Api` method). |
| **Mutation testing scout** | `.cargo/mutants.toml` skip entries + new positive test | Ran `cargo mutants -p ars-components -f .../highlight.rs`. Initial run: **4 missed / 61 total**. Of those: one real test gap (`Api::ignore_case` accessor could be hardcoded to `false` with no test failing), three equivalent mutations (defensive zero-length-range guards). Fixed real gap with `api_ignore_case_returns_both_directions`; documented equivalents in `mutants.toml`. **Re-run: 57/57 caught, 0 missed.** |
| **ars-i18n exports `case_fold`** with 4 new tests | `crates/ars-i18n/src/case.rs`, `lib.rs` | Eszett-expansion, Greek final-sigma collapse, Turkic-vs-non-Turkic fold divergence, fold idempotence. Gated on `feature = "icu4x"`. |
| **Spec core wording updated** | `spec/components/utility/highlight.md` §1.2 doc-comment | Now describes case **folding** (TR21) rather than locale-aware lowercase, with concrete examples for eszett, Greek sigma, Turkic. |

## Anything still left?

I genuinely don't think so. I checked:

- Spec-impl drift — fixed.
- Adapter spec alignment — fixed.
- Adapter feature wiring — fixed.
- Catalog and manifest entries — already present.
- Roundtrip + per-strategy invariants — covered (unit + proptest).
- Case fold semantics — actually correct now (was a silent contract violation before).
- Foundation pattern documentation — added.
- Mutation testing — clean (0 missed).
- Prelude — out of scope (no Leptos/Dioxus Highlight component yet; lands with the adapter task).
- Performance — current algorithm is correct; ICU4X 2.x has no streaming case-fold API, so the per-char allocation is the only way to maintain byte-mapping precision.
- BiDi — `dir="auto"` is in core; `unicode-bidi: isolate` is CSS-only, correctly left to adapter styling.

Ready for `cargo xci` + commit + PR whenever you sign off.

//! Spec-conformance tests for ars-components.
//!
//! Each module asserts that a component's `Part` enum matches the
//! anatomy table declared in `spec/components/.../{component}.md` §3.
//! The test layer is intentionally tiny — it leans on the derive-generated
//! `Part::scope()`, `Part::all()`, and `Part::name()` to compare against a
//! hand-rolled allow-list of `(Part, expected-kebab-name)` pairs per
//! component.
//!
//! New components SHOULD add a sibling module here (or extend an existing
//! one) so any drift between the spec's declared parts and the impl is
//! caught at build time, not by a reviewer's eye.
//!
//! See `spec/foundation/10-component-spec-template.md` "Spec-Conformance
//! Test (all tiers)" for the workspace convention.

#[path = "spec_conformance/helper.rs"]
mod helper;

#[path = "spec_conformance/navigation.rs"]
mod navigation;

#[path = "spec_conformance/overlay.rs"]
mod overlay;
---
helper.rs
navigation.rs
overlay.rs
```

1	//! Shared helpers for spec-conformance tests.
2	//!
3	//! Per-component tests call [`assert_anatomy`] with the expected scope and
4	//! the expected `(part, kebab-name)` list. The helper checks every facet
5	//! of the `ComponentPart` contract that the spec's §3 anatomy table
6	//! declares:
7	//!
8	//! 1. `Part::scope()` matches the spec's `data-ars-scope` token.
9	//! 2. `Part::all()` produces exactly the declared parts in the declared
10	//!    order.
11	//! 3. Every declared part round-trips through `Part::name()` with the
12	//!    declared kebab-case token.
13	//! 4. `Part::ROOT` equals the first declared part (workspace convention:
14	//!    every `Part` enum's first variant is `Root`).
15	
16	use ars_core::ComponentPart;
17	
18	/// Asserts that the supplied `Part` enum matches the spec's anatomy
19	/// declaration.
20	///
21	/// `expected_scope` is the `#[scope = "..."]` token the spec puts on the
22	/// `data-ars-scope` attribute. `expected_parts` is the ordered list of
23	/// `(part, kebab-name)` pairs from the spec's §3 anatomy table.
24	pub(crate) fn assert_anatomy<P>(expected_scope: &'static str, expected_parts: &[(P, &'static str)])
25	where
26	    P: ComponentPart + core::fmt::Debug + PartialEq,
27	{
28	    assert_eq!(
29	        P::scope(),
30	        expected_scope,
31	        "Part::scope() must match spec §3 anatomy",
32	    );
33	
34	    let actual = P::all();
35	
36	    let actual_names = actual.iter().map(ComponentPart::name).collect::<Vec<_>>();
37	
38	    let expected_names = expected_parts
39	        .iter()
40	        .map(|(_, name)| *name)
41	        .collect::<Vec<_>>();
42	
43	    assert_eq!(
44	        actual_names, expected_names,
45	        "Part::all() name list must equal spec §3 anatomy column \
46	         (scope = {expected_scope:?})",
47	    );
48	
49	    assert_eq!(
50	        actual.len(),
51	        expected_parts.len(),
52	        "Part::all() length must match the number of spec §3 anatomy rows",
53	    );
54	
55	    for (actual_part, (expected_part, expected_name)) in actual.iter().zip(expected_parts) {
56	        assert_eq!(
57	            actual_part.name(),
58	            *expected_name,
59	            "Part {actual_part:?} must produce kebab-name {expected_name:?}",
60	        );
61	        assert_eq!(
62	            actual_part, expected_part,
63	            "Part::all()[i] must equal the expected part value",
64	        );
65	    }
66	
67	    if let Some((first_expected, _)) = expected_parts.first() {
68	        assert_eq!(
69	            &P::ROOT,
70	            first_expected,
71	            "Workspace convention: Part::ROOT (the first variant) must \
72	             equal the first spec-declared part",
73	        );
74	    }
75	}
76	
```

# Test coverage assessment for Highlight

## Quantitative summary

| Test type | Count | What it covers | Effective coverage |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Unit tests** | 38 | Each `MatchStrategy`, both `ignore_case` directions, all edge cases (empty query/text, query>text, all-empty queries), Props builder + derives, Api accessors, AttrMap helpers, Turkic & German case folding | High |
| **Snapshot tests (insta)** | 9 | `root_attrs` (includes `dir="auto"`); `chunk_attrs(true/false)`; `highlight_chunks` for Contains, StartsWith, Fuzzy, multi-query merged, Turkic, German eszett | Good — covers all strategies + key locales |
| **Property tests (proptest)** | 1 block, 5 invariants | (props × locale) fuzz over 1000 cases (10000 nightly). Asserts: never panics; chunks roundtrip text; no two adjacent highlighted; no empty chunks; empty-query/empty-text edge cases | Strong — exercises a broad input space |
| **Mutation tests (cargo-mutants)** | 57/57 caught | All algorithmic branches in `push_*_matches`, `merge_ranges`, `build_chunks`, `build_normalised`, Api accessors, attribute emission | Best-case |
| **Code coverage (cargo llvm-cov)** | 98.67% lines / 100% functions / 99.16% regions | Per-line / per-region coverage of `highlight.rs` | Above workspace 90/90 threshold |
| **Spec-conformance tests** | 0 for Highlight | Workspace pattern (`tests/spec_conformance/*.rs`) exists for tabs and toast | **Missing for Highlight** |
| **Doc tests** | 0 | No `# Examples` in `///` blocks | **Missing** — could add canonical usage example |
| **E2E / browser tests** | N/A | Agnostic core has no DOM dependency; browser tests land with Leptos/Dioxus adapter | Correctly absent |

## Specific gaps the coverage report and proptest don't catch

I traced the 1.33% line gap and 0.84% region gap to four concrete spots:

### Gap A — dead `continue` branch (line 336)
```rust
if normalised_query.is_empty() {
    continue;  // <- 0 hits
}
```
Reached only if `case_fold(non_empty)` returns empty. Per Unicode TR21, every assigned character folds to a non-empty replacement, so this is unreachable code. The check at the top of the loop (`non_empty_queries` filter at line 287) already guarantees non-empty input. **Recommendation: remove.** Clean code + 100% line coverage.

### Gap B — `merge_ranges` no-update branch (line 502 else)
```rust
if end > last.1 {
    last.1 = end;
}
// else: contained range, no update — never tested
```
Triggers when range 2 is *fully contained* within range 1. Requires multi-query with a query that matches a substring of another query's match. **No existing test exercises this.** Concrete missing test: `query=["foobar", "ooba"]`, `text="foobar"` — both match overlapping; the second range is fully inside the first.

### Gap C — `push_starts_with_match` zero-length-range filter (line 446 else)
```rust
if orig_end > 0 {
    out.push((0, orig_end));
}
// else: orig_end == 0 (partial fold-expansion match) — never tested
```
Triggers when StartsWith matches the prefix of a fold-expansion that doesn't cross an original-char boundary. Concrete case: text `"İa"` under en-US (fold `"İ" → "i\u{307}"`), query `"i"`, StartsWith strategy — folded text starts with `"i"` but maps to a zero-length original range. The filter correctly skips it. **No existing test exercises this defense.**

### Gap D — `push_contains_matches` zero-length filter (analogous to C)
Same scenario, Contains strategy. Existing tests don't exercise the partial-fold case.

## Gaps in test-type breadth (not coverage)

| Gap | Why it matters | Effort to add |
|---|---|---|
| **No spec-conformance test** for Highlight in `tests/spec_conformance/utility.rs` | Workspace convention (`foundation/10` conformance checklist) calls for one. Catches drift between the spec's §2 anatomy table and the `Part` enum at build time, not via reviewer eye. Currently only tabs and toast have one. | ~10 LOC |
| **No doc test** showing canonical usage | `# Examples` blocks under `///` double as living documentation + verification. The spec template's "Spec-Conformance Test" item suggests at least one. | ~10 LOC |
| **No idempotence-under-duplicate-queries test** | `query=["foo", "foo"]` and `query=["foo"]` should produce identical output. Catches accidental side effects in the per-query loop. | ~5 LOC |
| **Proptest locale matrix is thin** (5 locales) | Covers en-US, tr, de, el, ar. Missing language families with case-fold edge cases (Lithuanian `lt` with combining dot, Armenian `hy`). | ~3 lines to add |
| **No long-text smoke** | No assertion that a paragraph-sized text finishes quickly. Catches accidental quadratic blow-up in the byte-map construction. | ~10 LOC |

## What I'd land vs. leave

| Landing now | Why |
|---|---|
| Remove dead `continue` branch (Gap A) | Reduces surface area, lifts line coverage to 100%, kills future "what is this for?" reviewer question |
| Add multi-query-fully-contained test (Gap B) | Real branch + real code path; missing test = future regression risk |
| Add partial-fold-expansion test (Gaps C+D, one test for both) | Documents the byte-map zero-length defense; future readers see what it's guarding against |
| Add spec-conformance test in `tests/spec_conformance/utility.rs` | Workspace convention; build-time anatomy-drift detection; ~10 LOC |
| Add doc test on `highlight_chunks` | Canonical example doubles as docs + verification; tiny |
| Add idempotence-under-duplicate-queries test | Useful invariant; ~5 LOC |
| Add 2 more proptest locales (`lt`, `hy`) | Broadens fuzz space at zero runtime cost |

| Leaving alone | Why |
|---|---|
| Long-text performance smoke | Would catch quadratic blow-up but ICU4X's `fold_string` is linear and our pipeline is O(n); premature without a complaint |
| `Hash`/`Copy`/`Eq` derive sanity tests | The derives are auto-generated; testing them tests the compiler, not us |
| Mutation-testing the `Api` accessor return-type variants | Already caught by `api_ignore_case_returns_both_directions` and friends |

## My recommendation

Land the 7 improvements in the first table. They're a combined ~80 LOC, all low-risk, all increase the surface that fails-loud on regressions. None changes behavior. Want me to proceed?

Catches regressions that would, e.g.,\n        // double-count ranges before the merge pass.\n        let single = Props::new().query([\"foo\"]).text(\"foobar foo\");\n        let duplicated = Props::new().query([\"foo\", \"foo\", \"foo\"]).text(\"foobar foo\");\n\n        assert_eq!(\n            highlight_chunks(&single, &en_us()),\n            highlight_chunks(&duplicated, &en_us())\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fold_expansion_partial_match_does_not_emit_partial_char_highlight() {\n        // Branch coverage for the byte-map zero-length-range filter in\n        // both `push_contains_matches` (orig_start < orig_end) and\n        // `push_starts_with_match` (orig_end > 0). ## Round-four improvements landed

| Improvement | Where | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| **Removed dead `continue` branch** | `highlight.rs` per-query loop | Cleans up unreachable code (case_fold never returns empty for non-empty input per TR21); kills future "what is this guarding?" reviewer questions |
| **`multi_query_fully_contained_range_does_not_extend`** | inline unit test | Covers the previously-unhit `merge_ranges` else branch: incoming range fully inside an existing range → no-op |
| **`multi_query_idempotent_under_duplicate_queries`** | inline unit test | Asserts `query=["foo","foo","foo"]` produces identical output to `query=["foo"]` — defends against accidental per-query side effects |
| **`fold_expansion_partial_match_does_not_emit_partial_char_highlight`** | inline unit test | Covers both `push_contains_matches`'s `orig_start < orig_end` filter AND `push_starts_with_match`'s `orig_end > 0` filter (parameterised over both strategies). Documents the byte-map zero-length-range defense with a real reproducer (en-US fold of `İ → i\u{307}` against query `"i"`) |
| **Doc test on `highlight_chunks`** | `///` block on the public function | Canonical usage example that doubles as living documentation + verification (`cargo test --doc`) |
| **2 more proptest locales (`az`, `lt`, `hy`)** | `tests/proptest_state_machines/utility.rs` | Broader fuzz space: Azerbaijani (Turkic family), Lithuanian (combining-dot tailoring), Armenian (cased script with no special CLDR tailoring) — at zero runtime cost vs. the existing 5 |
| **Spec-conformance test for Highlight** | new file `tests/spec_conformance/utility.rs` registered in `tests/spec_conformance.rs` | Asserts `Part::scope() == "highlight"` and `Part::all() == [Root]` against the spec's §2 anatomy table. Build-time anatomy-drift detection per workspace convention (matches the tabs / toast tests) |

The single remaining "uncovered" line is the defensive `else` of `if end > start` in `build_chunks` — a guard against zero-length highlighted chunks that no upstream pipeline stage can produce. Already documented as an equivalent mutation in `.cargo/mutants.toml` with full justification (and cargo-mutants confirms 0 missed). Driving this to literal 100.00% would require removing the guard (which would make a future regression silent) or adding a misleading test that fakes inputs to reach it. **99.91% with full mutation coverage is the right ceiling.**

Sometimes the best outcome may the spec, other times it will be the current implementation, some other times it will be a mix of both, and eventually it can also be a totally different thing.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/.claude/skills/post-implementation-audit/SKILL.md",
  "content": "---\nname: post-implementation-audit\ndescription: MANDATORY after every implementation task — runs three sequential audits (spec drift, iterative \"anything missing?\", test coverage) on the new code and lands every finding in the same PR before the user-review step of the CLAUDE.md workflow. Trigger whenever an implementation task has just had its named tests pass and you are about to present the result to the user. Also trigger on phrases like \"ready to present\", \"implementation is complete\", \"named tests pass\", \"before opening the PR\", or \"ready for review\". Skipping this skill leaves spec drift, untested defensive code, missing conventions, and silent contract violations in the merged PR — bugs that took the user three round-trips to catch on task #204 will all merge silently if this audit is not run.\n---\n\n# Post-implementation audit\n\nThis skill is the bridge between *\"the named tests pass\"* and *\"the user reviews the diff\"*. It exists because the initial implementation of any task is usually correct on its surface acceptance criteria but quietly drifts from the spec, leaves untested defensive code, ships APIs that diverge from convention, or carries a fold-vs-lowercase mistake that won't surface until production. Running these audits before user-review collapses three reviewer round-trips into one.\n\nThe skill is mandatory per CLAUDE.md \"Development Workflow\" step 7 — run it after implementing any user-requested task (feature, bug fix, refactor) and before presenting the final result for user review.\n\n## Operating principle: no deferral\n\n**Every recommendation each audit phase produces lands in *this* PR.** Don't open follow-up issues. Don't TODO-flag. Don't promise \"next iteration\". Don't quietly skip a finding because it feels like scope creep.\n\nIf a finding is genuinely out of scope (e.g., requires a workspace-wide refactor touching 50+ files), surface it explicitly to the user with that reasoning visible — but the default is to land everything. This constraint is what makes the audit valuable: a deferred finding is a guarantee that the original implementation's bias survives into `main`, where it costs ten times as much to undo.\n\nThe user will tell you \"land it all\". Anticipate that, and just do it.\n\n## Three sequential phases\n\nRun the phases **in order**. Each phase fully completes (audit → land all findings → re-verify) before the next begins.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 1 — spec/implementation drift audit\n\nCompare the implementation against the authoritative spec (`spec/components/<category>/<component>.md` plus any foundation specs it transitively depends on) section-by-section. For every drift, propose the **best outcome independent of which side is currently right**. Sometimes the spec wins, sometimes the impl wins, sometimes the right answer is neither.\n\nThe canonical entry-prompt for this phase (use this wording when the user asks you to start the audit explicitly):\n\n> \"Now let's do an audit on how well our new code follows our spec. Do an in-depth check for spec drift and suggest recommendations for each finding. In those recommendations, disregard both the spec and the current implementation as defaults — the recommendation should be the best outcome possible. Sometimes that's the spec, sometimes the implementation, sometimes a mix, sometimes a totally different thing.\"\n\n#### Procedure\n\n1. Read the spec file end-to-end (use `Read` with no offset/limit so you have the whole thing).\n2. Read the implementation file end-to-end (same).\n3. Walk every public surface declared in the spec — every struct, enum, function, method, derive list, default value, constant, public re-export. For each:\n   - Does the spec define it? Does the impl match exactly?\n   - Are there extras in the impl not in the spec? Are they justified?\n   - Are there spec items the impl skipped? Are they critical?\n   - **Does the spec's prose match the impl's actual behavior?** This is the silent-contract-violation class — words that mean something specific in the spec but were implemented as something close-but-different (e.g., \"case folding\" in the spec but `to_lowercase` in the impl).\n4. For each finding, write up:\n   - What the spec says, with line numbers\n   - What the impl does, with line numbers\n   - Why it drifted (often a tradeoff the implementer made under acceptance-criteria pressure)\n   - **Best-outcome recommendation** — independent of which side is currently right\n5. Group findings by severity (High / Medium / Low) and present as a table to the user.\n6. Land all of them. Update the spec where the impl is right, update the impl where the spec is right, do both where neither was right, change *only* the spec wording (and add tests proving the new wording) when the contract was subtly wrong all along.\n\n#### Output table format\n\n| Finding ID | Severity | What spec says | What impl does | Best outcome | Where to land |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| H1 | High | … | … | … | spec §X / impl `path:line` |\n\nEnd with a disposition summary: *\"N spec-only changes, M impl-only changes, K both, Q status-quo-was-correct.\"*\n\n---\n\n### Phase 2 — iterative \"anything else missing?\" passes\n\nOnce Phase 1 findings are landed, the next blind spot is the surface Phase 1 didn't look at: adapter specs, feature flags, prelude exports, framework wiring, foundation patterns that deserve promotion, catalog/manifest entries. This phase is **iterative** — keep asking \"anything else?\" until two consecutive rounds find nothing new.\n\nThe canonical entry-prompt (one per round):\n\n> \"Any other improvement that we might still be missing?\"\n\n#### Surfaces to actively check each round\n\nThis is a non-exhaustive checklist. Each round, walk through it explicitly:\n\n- **Adapter specs** at `spec/leptos-components/<category>/<component>.md` and `spec/dioxus-components/<category>/<component>.md` — do they describe the new core surface accurately? Are their canonical implementation sketches up-to-date with the actual `Api` / function signatures? Do they reference the right helper methods (e.g., `Api::root_attrs()`) instead of hardcoded data attribute strings?\n- **Adapter feature wiring** in `crates/ars-leptos/Cargo.toml` and `crates/ars-dioxus/Cargo.toml` — does the adapter's `icu4x` / `web-intl` feature need to enable any new `ars-components/<feature>` flag for the new code to be reachable?\n- **Adapter `prelude.rs`** — does the new public API need to flow through? (Only if an adapter wrapper exists for the new component; if not, defer to the adapter task.)\n- **Workspace `spec/manifest.toml`** and **`foundation/02-component-catalog.md`** — is the new component registered and statused? (For brand-new components only.)\n- **Foundation specs** (`spec/foundation/00-*` through `spec/foundation/11-*`) — does the new code expose a missing shared abstraction worth promoting? CLAUDE.md `spec/CLAUDE.md` says: *\"If an adapter-specific implementation exposes a missing shared abstraction, promote that abstraction into the appropriate foundation/shared spec.\"*\n- **New crate dependencies** — was the user notified per CLAUDE.md's \"Do not add a new dependency crate without explicit user approval first\" rule? Did the implementation add `i18n`-style feature flags that warrant a CLAUDE.md note?\n- **`.cargo/mutants.toml`** — are any new equivalent mutations documented with justifications? (Phase 3 will validate this; surface it here if a mutation that *should* be equivalent isn't documented yet.)\n\n#### Termination\n\nStop when:\n\n- Two consecutive rounds surface no new findings, **OR**\n- Three rounds have been completed and the remaining findings are honestly out-of-scope (genuine workspace-wide refactors that would derail this PR).\n\nDo **not** stop after one clean round — the second round nearly always surfaces items the first ignored because they felt out-of-scope. With the \"no deferral\" rule active, they're back in scope.\n\n#### Output per round\n\nA list of findings with the same \"best outcome\" framing as Phase 1, plus an explicit statement at the end: *\"That's everything I can find this round. Want me to do another?\"* — knowing the answer will be yes for at least one more round.\n\n---\n\n### Phase 3 — test coverage audit\n\nBy this point the code is convention-aligned and well-specced. Now check the **test surface** — both depth (line / branch / mutation coverage) and breadth (does every test type that *should* exist for this kind of change exist?).\n\nThe canonical entry-prompt:\n\n> \"How good is our test coverage here, by looking at the code paths, checking the coverage report and considering the many kinds of tests that we are intending to have (including snapshot tests, proptests, and e2e tests)? Can it be improved?\"\n\n#### Test types and what each catches\n\n| Test type | What it catches | How to assess for the new code |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Unit tests** (inline `#[cfg(test)]`) | Logic + edge cases on the named acceptance criteria | List every public API + every edge case the spec describes; cross-reference with the test list |\n| **Snapshot tests** (insta) | `AttrMap` / chunk-output shape regressions | Check coverage of every anatomy part and every output-affecting prop / state / context branch |\n| **Property tests** (proptest) | Invariant violations across the input space | Check that invariants are non-trivial (roundtrip, idempotence, no-adjacent-X, etc.) and the input strategy is broad enough to actually exercise edge locales / multi-byte / large inputs |\n| **Mutation tests** (cargo-mutants) | Tests that *exist* but don't actually constrain behavior | Run `cargo mutants -p <crate> -f <file>` and triage every `MISSED` |\n| **Doc tests** | Public-API examples broken by future changes | Check `///` blocks for at least one `# Examples` block on the canonical entry point |\n| **Spec-conformance tests** | Anatomy drift between spec and impl | Check `crates/ars-components/tests/spec_conformance/*.rs` for an entry covering the new component's `Part` enum |\n| **Code coverage** (cargo llvm-cov) | Unreachable / under-tested code | Run `cargo llvm-cov ... --text` and look at `^0` branch markers |\n| **E2E / browser tests** | DOM-level behavior in the adapter | N/A for agnostic-core changes; required if the change touches `ars-leptos` or `ars-dioxus` adapter code via the adapter test harnesses |\n\n#### Procedure\n\n1. **Coverage report.** Run:\n   ```bash\n   cargo llvm-cov test -p <crate> --features <relevant> --lib --no-fail-fast --text -- <module>::\n   ```\n   Read the per-line annotated output. Every `^0` marker is an unhit branch — investigate each one. Categorize as: real test gap / equivalent defensive guard / unreachable dead code.\n\n2. **Mutation testing.** Run:\n   ```bash\n   cargo mutants -p <crate> -f <file>\n   ```\n   For each `MISSED` line, decide:\n   - **Real test gap** → add a test that kills the mutation (often a positive-direction assertion the existing tests skipped).\n   - **Equivalent mutation** → add a regex to `.cargo/mutants.toml` `exclude_re` with a written justification explaining *why* the mutation cannot change observable behavior.\n   - **Defensive code that's truly unreachable** → consider deleting the code. This is often the cleanest fix and is the right answer for unreachable post-filter guards.\n\n3. **Test-type breadth audit.** Walk the table above. For each test type, ask: *\"Does this kind of change need this kind of test, and do we have one?\"* Examples:\n   - New utility component with an anatomy table → spec-conformance test required\n   - New public function → doc test with `# Examples` required\n   - New invariants on a computation pipeline → proptest invariants strongly recommended\n   - New `AttrMap` helpers → snapshot tests for each branch required\n\n4. **Cumulative assessment.** Present the before/after metrics in a table:\n\n| Metric | Before this phase | After this phase |\n|---|---|---|\n| Lines covered | x% | y% |\n| Mutations caught | x/total | y/total |\n| Unit tests | N | M |\n| Doc tests | N | M |\n| Spec-conformance test | yes/no | yes/no |\n\n#### Output\n\nA list of gaps with concrete reproducers (specific test inputs that trigger the missed branch). For each gap, classify the disposition (real-gap-add-test / equivalent-mutation-skip / dead-code-remove). End with a clear *\"land all\"* and the verification commands to confirm.\n\n#### Anti-pattern: chasing 100% line coverage\n\nThe goal is **not** \"100% line coverage at any cost\". The goal is *\"no surviving mutations, no unreachable defensive code, every test type that should exist does exist\"*. A 99% line-coverage report with all mutations caught is a stronger position than a 100% line-coverage report with three surviving mutations. If the last 1% of uncovered code is a genuinely unreachable defensive guard, document it in `mutants.toml` and move on.\n\n---\n\n## After the three phases\n\nRun the full verification suite one final time before handing off to the user:\n\n```bash\n# 1. Unit + integration tests, both feature-on and feature-off variants\ncargo test -p <crate> --features <relevant> --all-targets\ncargo test -p <crate> --lib                       # without the optional features\n\n# 2. Workspace clippy (must be -D warnings clean)\ncargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --exclude ars-i18n -- -D warnings\n\n# 3. Backend matrices for any feature-gated code\ncargo clippy -p ars-i18n --no-default-features --features std,icu4x --all-targets -- -D warnings\ncargo clippy -p ars-i18n --no-default-features --features std,web-intl --all-targets -- -D warnings\n\n# 4. Docs build (0 new warnings on the changed crate)\ncargo doc -p <crate> --no-deps --all-features\n\n# 5. Spec validation + snippet check\ncargo xtask spec validate\ncargo test -p xtask --test spec_corpus_compile_snippets\n\n# 6. Snapshot orphan check\ncargo insta test --workspace --features <relevant> --unreferenced=reject\n\n# 7. Mutation re-run (must be 0 missed)\ncargo mutants -p <crate> -f <file>\n\n# 8. Adapter builds reachable with feature wiring\ncargo build -p ars-leptos --features icu4x\ncargo build -p ars-dioxus --features icu4x,web\n```\n\nThen write **one consolidated user summary** covering all three phases' findings + the verification table. Hand off for user review per CLAUDE.md workflow step 8 (which was the old step 7 before this skill was inserted).\n\n## Anti-patterns\n\n- **Deferring** any finding (\"we can fix that in a follow-up\"). This is the rule the user set explicitly. Breaking it makes the audit ceremonial.\n- **Loading the user with options mid-phase.** Make a clear best-outcome recommendation. Only escalate to `AskUserQuestion` when there's a genuine judgment call the user must make (e.g., behavior choices with real tradeoffs, not implementation details).\n- **Skipping rounds of Phase 2** because the first round was clean. The second round nearly always finds something the first ignored.\n- **Treating coverage % as the goal.** The goal is mutation-killed and breadth-complete, not a number.\n- **Inventing cosmetic edits** the audits didn't surface. If a phase doesn't find a real finding, don't fabricate one — say \"this round found nothing\" and proceed.\n- **Re-running tests after every micro-edit** during a phase. Land a batch of related findings, then verify, then move on.\n- **Forgetting to update the named-tests file** when a new test exposes a real bug fixed in the same phase. The fix and the test land together.\n\n## What this skill is not\n\n- A code-review skill. Code review (style, naming, simplification) belongs to `pr-review-toolkit:code-reviewer` and runs after the PR is opened.\n- A pre-implementation skill. Before implementing, `feature-dev:feature-dev` and `superpowers:writing-plans` handle planning.\n- A way to revisit acceptance criteria. The acceptance criteria are fixed by the GitHub issue. This skill assumes they're already met.\n\n## Worked example — task #204\n\nThe flow this skill encodes was validated on GitHub issue #204 (`task: Implement Highlight agnostic core`). The initial implementation passed all 8 named acceptance-criterion tests on first try. Then:\n\n- **Phase 1** surfaced 16 drift findings, including a silent contract violation (the spec said *\"Unicode case folding\"* but the impl used `to_lowercase` — they aren't equivalent: under `to_lowercase`, query `\"ss\"` does not match text `\"ß\"`, violating the spec's explicit eszett contract). Fix: add `ars_i18n::case_fold`, switch the matching pipeline, rewrite the German test to assert the actual three-direction equivalence.\n- **Phase 2 round 1** surfaced 4 more items (adapter spec alignment, adapter feature wiring, roundtrip invariant test, proptest invariants).\n- **Phase 2 round 2** surfaced 3 more (a `case_fold` vs `to_lowercase` semantic gap, foundation/10 parametric-anatomy concept, cargo-mutants scout).\n- **Phase 3** surfaced 4 coverage gaps (dead `continue` branch, `merge_ranges` else branch, fold-expansion partial-match defense, spec-conformance test). Pushed line coverage from 98.67% to 99.91% with all 57 mutations caught.\n\nThe cumulative effect: PR landed with one user review pass instead of three, and the spec contract (`ß ↔ ss`) is actually true instead of cosmetically true. That's the value this skill creates.\n"
}
```

18	
19	## Development Workflow
20	
21	For implementation tasks:
22	
23	1. Read the assigned or selected GitHub issue first.
24	2. Move the issue to **In Progress** on the GitHub Project board.
25	3. Review the cited spec sections and any dependency issues.
26	4. Add or update the named tests first.
27	5. Implement only the scope required to make those tests pass.
28	6. If implementation changes the intended contract, update the relevant spec in the same task.
29	7. Present the final result for user review before any commit.
30	8. After user approval, run `cargo xci` locally and fix any failures before pushing anything to GitHub. During normal implementation, reserve `cargo xci` for substantial chunks of code or the final pre-PR gate; for small follow-up changes, run the focused tests and checks that cover the edited code instead.
31	9. After `cargo xci` passes, commit and push a PR targeting `main`. The PR body MUST include an auto-close keyword for the issue being delivered, for example `Closes #20` or `Fixes #20`, so GitHub closes the issue automatically when the PR merges.
32	10. **If the PR creates or modifies any `.snap` insta fixtures, attach the `snapshot-reviewed` label after opening or updating it** (`gh pr edit <num> --add-label "snapshot-reviewed"`). This signals to reviewers that the snapshot output was inspected and is intentional. Re-apply the label whenever you push a commit that touches `.snap` files; the workflow assumes the label reflects the latest snapshot state.
33	11. Only after CI passes and the PR is merged, close the issue.
34	
35	Never close a GitHub issue without a merged PR that passes CI. Never commit or push without explicit user approval. Keep the issue, PR, and Project board status aligned with the actual work state at every step.
36	
37	Default delivery rules:
38	
39	- Follow TDD: tests first, implementation second, verification last.
40	- Keep task scope aligned with the issue. If the issue is too large or ambiguous, stop and split or clarify rather than freelancing a bigger change.
41	- Prefer tasks sized `1`, `2`, `3`, or `5` points. `8` is exceptional. `13` must be decomposed before implementation.
42	- Preserve the crate and dependency layering defined by the architecture and implementation plan.
43	- Do not add a new dependency crate without explicit user approval first. If a task appears to need a new crate, stop, explain why, and get approval before editing any `Cargo.toml`.
44	- When a task is complete, verify the exact tests and checks named by the issue before considering it done.
45	
46	### Code Quality Standards
47	
48	- **Document all public items.** Every public struct, enum, trait, function, constant, type alias, method, field, and variant must have a `///` doc comment. Every `lib.rs` must have a `//!` crate-level doc comment. The workspace enforces `missing_docs` linting — undocumented public items are build failures.
49	- **Zero warnings policy.** All code must compile with zero warnings under the workspace's configured clippy and rustc lints. Fix the root cause instead of suppressing. When suppression is genuinely needed, use `#[expect(lint, reason = "...")]` — never `#[allow(...)]`.
50	- **Derive documentation from the spec.** Doc comments should describe the _purpose and semantics_ of the item as defined in the corresponding `spec/` files, not just restate the type signature.
51	- **Use `#[inline]` selectively, not mechanically.** Do **not** add Clippy's `missing_inline_in_public_items` lint at the workspace level, and do not treat public visibility alone as a reason to mark an item `#[inline]`. Use `#[inline]` for thin cross-crate wrappers, trivial accessors, and hot-path no-op shims where the call overhead is plausibly meaningful. Avoid blanket `#[inline]` on all public APIs — it increases code size, adds compile-time cost, and turns `#[inline]` into noise instead of a deliberate performance signal.
52	- **Use directory-backed modules with `mod.rs` when a module owns children.** This repo standardizes on the older filesystem layout for nested modules. If module `foo` has child modules, the parent must live at `foo/mod.rs`, not `foo.rs`. Do not mix `foo.rs` with a sibling `foo/` directory. When a refactor adds child modules under an existing flat file, move the parent into `foo/mod.rs` in the same change. Do not leave empty leftover module directories behind.

=== CLAUDE.md / AGENTS.md workflow section ===
For implementation tasks:

1. Read the assigned or selected GitHub issue first.
2. Move the issue to **In Progress** on the GitHub Project board.
3. Review the cited spec sections and any dependency issues.
4. Add or update the named tests first.
5. Implement only the scope required to make those tests pass.
6. If implementation changes the intended contract, update the relevant spec in the same task.
7. **MANDATORY:** Invoke the `post-implementation-audit` skill (`.claude/skills/post-implementation-audit/SKILL.md`). It runs three sequential audits on the new code — (a) spec/implementation drift with "best outcome" recommendations, (b) iterative "anything else missing?" passes (minimum two rounds), (c) test-coverage audit across every test type the workspace uses (unit, snapshot, proptest, mutation, doc, spec-conformance, llvm-cov). **Every finding lands in *this* PR — no deferral, no follow-up issues.** This step exists because initial implementations consistently ship spec drift and silent contract violations that take 3+ review round-trips to surface; the audit collapses them into one. Skipping this step is a contract violation.
8. Present the final result for user review before any commit.
9. After user approval, run `cargo xci` locally and fix any failures before pushing anything to GitHub. During normal implementation, reserve `cargo xci` for substantial chunks of code or the final pre-PR gate; for small follow-up changes, run the focused tests and checks that cover the edited code instead.
10. After `cargo xci` passes, commit and push a PR targeting `main`. The PR body MUST include an auto-close keyword for the issue being delivered, for example `Closes #20` or `Fixes #20`, so GitHub closes the issue automatically when the PR merges.
11. **If the PR creates or modifies any `.snap` insta fixtures, attach the `snapshot-reviewed` label after opening or updating it** (`gh pr edit <num> --add-label "snapshot-reviewed"`). This signals to reviewers that the snapshot output was inspected and is intentional. Re-apply the label whenever you push a commit that touches `.snap` files; the workflow assumes the label reflects the latest snapshot state.
12. Only after CI passes and the PR is merged, close the issue.

Never close a GitHub issue without a merged PR that passes CI. Never commit or push without explicit user approval. Keep the issue, PR, and Project board status aligned with the actual work state at every step.

Default delivery rules:
```

=== Frontmatter ===
---
name: post-implementation-audit
description: MANDATORY after every implementation task — runs three sequential audits (spec drift, iterative "anything missing?", test coverage) on the new code and lands every finding in the same PR before the user-review step of the CLAUDE.md workflow. Trigger whenever an implementation task has just had its named tests pass and you are about to present the result to the user. Also trigger on phrases like "ready to present", "implementation is complete", "named tests pass", "before opening the PR", or "ready for review". Skipping this skill leaves spec drift, untested defensive code, missing conventions, and silent contract violations in the merged PR — bugs that took the user three round-trips to catch on task #204 will all merge silently if this audit is not run.
---

# Post-implementation audit

This skill is the bridge between *"the named tests pass"* and *"the user reviews the diff"*. It exists because the initial implementation of any task is usually correct on its surface acceptance criteria but quietly drifts from the spec, leaves untested defensive code, ships APIs that diverge from convention, or carries a fold-vs-lowercase mistake that won't surface until production. Running these audits before user-review collapses three reviewer round-trips into one.

The skill is mandatory per CLAUDE.md "Development Workflow" step 7 — run it after implementing any user-requested task (feature, bug fix, refactor) and before presenting the final result for user review.

=== Section headings ===
12:## Operating principle: no deferral
20:## Three sequential phases
26:### Phase 1 — spec/implementation drift audit
61:### Phase 2 — iterative "anything else missing?" passes
96:### Phase 3 — test coverage audit
160:## After the three phases
196:## Anti-patterns
206:## What this skill is not
212:## Worked example — task #204
```

7. **MANDATORY:** Invoke the `post-implementation-audit` skill (`.claude/skills/post-implementation-audit/SKILL.md`). It runs three sequential audits on the new code — (a) spec/implementation drift with "best outcome" recommendations, (b) iterative "anything else missing?" passes (minimum two rounds), (c) test-coverage audit across every test type the workspace uses (unit, snapshot, proptest, mutation, doc, spec-conformance, llvm-cov). **Every finding lands in *this* PR — no deferral, no follow-up issues.** This step exists because initial implementations consistently ship spec drift and silent contract violations that take 3+ review round-trips to surface; the audit collapses them into one. Skipping this step is a contract violation.
8. Present the final result for user review before any commit.
9. After user approval, run `cargo xci` locally and fix any failures before pushing anything to GitHub. During normal implementation, reserve `cargo xci` for substantial chunks of code or the final pre-PR gate; for small follow-up changes, run the focused tests and checks that cover the edited code instead.
10. After `cargo xci` passes, commit and push a PR targeting `main`. The PR body MUST include an auto-close keyword for the issue being delivered, for example `Closes #20` or `Fixes #20`, so GitHub closes the issue automatically when the PR merges.
--
The workspace uses `cargo-llvm-cov` for code coverage with per-crate threshold enforcement. CI runs coverage on every PR (see `.github/workflows/ci.yml`).

**Local coverage commands:**

--
cargo llvm-cov test -p ars-interactions --text -- hover

# Per-crate summary only
cargo llvm-cov test -p ars-interactions -- hover

# Native-only workspace coverage (host target; excludes wasm-only crates)
cargo llvm-cov --workspace \
  --exclude ars-leptos --exclude ars-dioxus \
  --exclude ars-test-harness-leptos --exclude ars-test-harness-dioxus \
  --exclude ars-derive --exclude xtask \
--
cargo xtask coverage check-all --file lcov.info
```

The `--text` flag produces line-by-line annotated source showing hit counts and `^0` markers on uncovered branches — use this to identify gaps after writing tests. Uncovered no-op callback closures in tests (e.g., `|_| {}`) are expected and not a gap.
--
The local recipe above excludes the adapter and adapter-test-harness crates because their `target_arch = "wasm32"` / `feature = "web"` code paths can't be measured via host-target `cargo llvm-cov`. **CI runs an additional wasm-coverage pipeline on nightly** (`cargo xtask ci coverage`) that uses `wasm-bindgen-test`'s experimental coverage recipe with LLVM 22 / `clang-22` to emit lcov for `ars-leptos` (csr), `ars-dioxus` (web), `ars-dom` (web), `ars-i18n` (web-intl), and the adapter test harnesses, then merges with the native lcov via `cargo xtask coverage merge`. The per-crate thresholds in `xtask::coverage::default_thresholds()` are enforced against the merged file — including adapter targets (`ars-leptos` 74/55, `ars-dioxus` 77/70, `ars-test-harness-leptos` 60/0, `ars-test-harness-dioxus` 60/0). `ars-derive` (proc-macro, runs in compiler) is exercised via `crates/ars-core/tests/derive_contract.rs` and is unmeasured. `xtask` has its own enforced threshold (30/35) and is measured. See `spec/testing/14-ci.md` §2 for the full pipeline.

### Mutation Testing
```

Every `^0` marker is an unhit branch — investigate each one. Categorize as: real test gap / equivalent defensive guard / unreachable dead code.",
  "new_string": "#### Procedure\n\n1. **Coverage report.** The command depends on which crate the change touches — this workspace measures host-target and wasm32 paths separately because adapter code (`ars-leptos`, `ars-dioxus`, `ars-dom`, the `ars-i18n` `web-intl` path, the adapter test harnesses) has `target_arch = \"wasm32\"` / `feature = \"web\"` code that bare host-target `cargo llvm-cov` cannot reach.\n\n   **For native-only crates** (`ars-components`, `ars-collections`, `ars-core`, `ars-forms`, `ars-interactions`, `ars-a11y`, `xtask`):\n   ```bash\n   cargo llvm-cov test -p <crate> --features <relevant> --lib --no-fail-fast --text -- <module>::\n   ```\n\n   **For adapter crates / `web-intl` paths** (`ars-leptos`, `ars-dioxus`, `ars-dom`, `ars-test-harness-leptos`, `ars-test-harness-dioxus`, or `ars-i18n` when the change is in the `web-intl` feature gate):\n   ```bash\n   cargo xtask coverage wasm --package <crate> --features <relevant> --output <crate>.lcov.info\n   ```\n   This uses `wasm-bindgen-test`'s experimental coverage recipe with LLVM 22 / `clang-22` (the same recipe CI nightly runs via `cargo xtask ci coverage`). The bare `cargo llvm-cov` command will silently report `0.00%` for the wasm-gated code paths, which is *not* the same as having no coverage — it means *unmeasured*. Don't trust low numbers on adapter crates from the native path.\n\n   **For a change that touches both native and wasm paths** (e.g., a new ars-i18n helper consumed from both backends, or a refactor in `ars-dom` that affects native callers):\n   ```bash\n   # Generate both:\n   cargo llvm-cov --workspace --exclude ars-leptos --exclude ars-dioxus \\\n     --exclude ars-test-harness-leptos --exclude ars-test-harness-dioxus \\\n     --exclude ars-derive --exclude xtask \\\n     --lcov --output-path native.lcov.info\n   cargo xtask coverage wasm --package <wasm-package> --output wasm.lcov.info\n   # Merge without double-counting duplicate lines:\n   cargo xtask coverage merge --inputs native.lcov.info wasm.lcov.info --output merged.lcov.info\n   # Enforce per-crate spec-defined thresholds:\n   cargo xtask coverage check-all --file merged.lcov.info\n   ```\n\n   The per-crate thresholds (line% / branch%) are encoded in `xtask::coverage::default_thresholds()` and include the wasm targets. `cargo xtask coverage check-all` is the same gate CI enforces, so a green local run guarantees green CI on the coverage step.\n\n   Read the per-line annotated output from the `--text` invocations. Every `^0` marker is an unhit branch — investigate each one. Categorize as: real test gap / equivalent defensive guard / unreachable dead code. **Expected non-gaps:** no-op callback closures in tests (e.g., `|_| {}`) are intentionally uncovered and not a real gap — CLAUDE.md calls this out explicitly.\n\n   **Not measured by either path:** `ars-derive` (proc-macro, runs in the compiler — exercised indirectly via `crates/ars-core/tests/derive_contract.rs`). Don't expect coverage numbers here."
}

170	|---|---|---|
171	| Lines covered | x% | y% |
172	| Mutations caught | x/total | y/total |
