177	#### Output
178	
179	A list of gaps with concrete reproducers (specific test inputs that trigger the missed branch). For each gap, classify the disposition (real-gap-add-test / equivalent-mutation-skip / dead-code-remove). End with a clear *"land all"* and the verification commands to confirm.
180	
181	#### Anti-pattern: chasing 100% line coverage
182	
183	The goal is **not** "100% line coverage at any cost". The goal is *"no surviving mutations, no unreachable defensive code, every test type that should exist does exist"*. A 99% line-coverage report with all mutations caught is a stronger position than a 100% line-coverage report with three surviving mutations. If the last 1% of uncovered code is a genuinely unreachable defensive guard, document it in `mutants.toml` and move on.
184	
185	---
186	
187	## After the three phases
188	
189	Run the full verification suite one final time before handing off to the user:
190	
191	```bash
192	# 1. Unit + integration tests, both feature-on and feature-off variants
193	cargo test -p <crate> --features <relevant> --all-targets
194	cargo test -p <crate> --lib                       # without the optional features
195	
196	# 2. Workspace clippy (must be -D warnings clean)
197	cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --exclude ars-i18n -- -D warnings
198	
199	# 3. Backend matrices for any feature-gated code
200	cargo clippy -p ars-i18n --no-default-features --features std,icu4x --all-targets -- -D warnings
201	cargo clippy -p ars-i18n --no-default-features --features std,web-intl --all-targets -- -D warnings
202	
203	# 4. Docs build (0 new warnings on the changed crate)
204	cargo doc -p <crate> --no-deps --all-features
205	
206	# 5. Spec validation + snippet check
207	cargo xtask spec validate
208	cargo test -p xtask --test spec_corpus_compile_snippets
209

220	
221	Then write **one consolidated user summary** covering all three phases' findings + the verification table. Hand off for user review per CLAUDE.md workflow step 8 (which was the old step 7 before this skill was inserted).
222	
223	## Anti-patterns
224	
225	- **Deferring** any finding ("we can fix that in a follow-up"). This is the rule the user set explicitly. Breaking it makes the audit ceremonial.
226	- **Loading the user with options mid-phase.** Make a clear best-outcome recommendation. Only escalate to `AskUserQuestion` when there's a genuine judgment call the user must make (e.g., behavior choices with real tradeoffs, not implementation details).
227	- **Skipping rounds of Phase 2** because the first round was clean. The second round nearly always finds something the first ignored.
228	- **Treating coverage % as the goal.** The goal is mutation-killed and breadth-complete, not a number.
229	- **Inventing cosmetic edits** the audits didn't surface. If a phase doesn't find a real finding, don't fabricate one — say "this round found nothing" and proceed.
230	- **Re-running tests after every micro-edit** during a phase. Land a batch of related findings, then verify, then move on.
231	- **Forgetting to update the named-tests file** when a new test exposes a real bug fixed in the same phase. The fix and the test land together.
232	
233	## What this skill is not
234	
235	- A code-review skill. Code review (style, naming, simplification) belongs to `pr-review-toolkit:code-reviewer` and runs after the PR is opened.
236	- A pre-implementation skill. Before implementing, `feature-dev:feature-dev` and `superpowers:writing-plans` handle planning.
237	- A way to revisit acceptance criteria. The acceptance criteria are fixed by the GitHub issue. This skill assumes they're already met.
```

The bare `cargo llvm-cov` command will silently report `0.00%` for the wasm-gated code paths, which is *not* the same as having no coverage — it means *unmeasured*. Don't trust low numbers on adapter crates from the native path.\n\n   **For a change that touches both native and wasm paths** (e.g., a new ars-i18n helper consumed from both backends, or a refactor in `ars-dom` that affects native callers):\n   ```bash\n   # Generate both:\n   cargo llvm-cov --workspace --exclude ars-leptos --exclude ars-dioxus \\\n     --exclude ars-test-harness-leptos --exclude ars-test-harness-dioxus \\\n     --exclude ars-derive --exclude xtask \\\n     --lcov --output-path native.lcov.info\n   cargo xtask coverage wasm --package <wasm-package> --output wasm.lcov.info\n   # Merge without double-counting duplicate lines:\n   cargo xtask coverage merge --inputs native.lcov.info wasm.lcov.info --output merged.lcov.info\n   # Enforce per-crate spec-defined thresholds:\n   cargo xtask coverage check-all --file merged.lcov.info\n   ```",
  "new_string": "   **For adapter crates / `web-intl` paths** (`ars-leptos`, `ars-dioxus`, `ars-dom`, `ars-test-harness-leptos`, `ars-test-harness-dioxus`, or `ars-i18n` when the change is in the `web-intl` feature gate):\n   ```bash\n   cargo xtask coverage wasm \\\n     --package <crate> \\\n     --feature <feat1> --feature <feat2> \\\n     --file wasm.lcov.info\n   ```\n   (`--feature` is singular and repeatable, NOT `--features` — it mirrors cargo's `--feature` convention; use `--no-default-features` to drop defaults.) This uses `wasm-bindgen-test`'s experimental coverage recipe with LLVM 22 / `clang-22` (the same recipe CI nightly runs via `cargo xtask ci coverage`). The bare `cargo llvm-cov` command will silently report `0.00%` for the wasm-gated code paths, which is *not* the same as having no coverage — it means *unmeasured*. Don't trust low numbers on adapter crates from the native path.\n\n   **For a change that touches both native and wasm paths** (e.g., a new ars-i18n helper consumed from both backends, or a refactor in `ars-dom` that affects native callers):\n   ```bash\n   # Generate the native lcov (workspace, excluding wasm-only crates):\n   cargo llvm-cov --workspace \\\n     --exclude ars-leptos --exclude ars-dioxus \\\n     --exclude ars-test-harness-leptos --exclude ars-test-harness-dioxus \\\n     --exclude ars-derive --exclude xtask \\\n     --lcov --output-path native.lcov.info\n\n   # Generate the wasm lcov for each adapter target you touched:\n   cargo xtask coverage wasm --package <wasm-package> --feature <feat> --file wasm.lcov.info\n\n   # Merge without double-counting duplicate lines (each `--file` adds an input):\n   cargo xtask coverage merge \\\n     --file native.lcov.info \\\n     --file wasm.lcov.info \\\n     --output merged.lcov.info\n\n   # Enforce per-crate spec-defined thresholds (same gate CI runs):\n   cargo xtask coverage check-all --file merged.lcov.info\n   ```"
}

> AGENT

Skill is now 266 lines (still well under the 500-line target). The Phase 3 coverage section now correctly routes by crate:

| Crate touched | Coverage command |
|---|---|
| **Native-only** (`ars-components`, `ars-collections`, `ars-core`, `ars-forms`, `ars-interactions`, `ars-a11y`, `xtask`) | `cargo llvm-cov test -p <crate> --features ... --text -- <module>::` |
| **Adapter / wasm** (`ars-leptos`, `ars-dioxus`, `ars-dom`, adapter test harnesses, `ars-i18n` web-intl path) | `cargo xtask coverage wasm --package <crate> --feature <f> --file out.lcov.info` |
| **Both** (rare: ars-i18n helpers consumed cross-backend, ars-dom refactors with native callers) | Generate both, then `cargo xtask coverage merge --file native --file wasm --output merged.lcov.info` |
| **Threshold gate** (same one CI runs) | `cargo xtask coverage check-all --file <lcov>` |

I also fixed:
- **Wrong flag names** (was `--features <list>` and `--output`; actual API is `--feature <name>` repeated and `--file`) — verified against `cargo xtask coverage wasm --help`.
- **Misleading "0% means no coverage"** — added the explicit warning that bare `cargo llvm-cov` reports `0.00%` for wasm-gated paths because they're *unmeasured*, not actually uncovered. Easy to misread as a real gap and chase phantom tests.
- **E2E row** in the test-types table now explicitly says wasm-bindgen-test, references `cargo xtask test`, and tells you to trust the wasm-path coverage number not the host-target one.
- **Verification suite step 8** now references `cargo xtask coverage check-all` (the actual CI gate) instead of implying bare `cargo llvm-cov` is sufficient.
- **`ars-derive`** is now flagged as unmeasured by either path (proc-macro, runs in the compiler).

Good catch — under the old wording, a future audit of an `ars-leptos` change would have produced false-positive coverage gaps (host-target reports 0% for csr-only code) and missed real ones (the wasm path wasn't run at all).

# My take on the six findings

Honest assessment: **4 of 6 are worth landing, 2 of 6 are the linter applying generic templates that don't fit a workflow skill of this kind.** Let me go through each.

---

### Finding 1 — "trigger phrases are ambiguous" (severity 4) → ✅ minor fix worth landing

**Linter's take:** "It is unclear whether all these phrases must be present, or if any one of them suffices."

**My take:** Half-valid. The current wording "*Also trigger on phrases like X, Y, Z*" is interpreted correctly by any model with normal English comprehension — "or" lists are disjunctive. But the linter has a point that explicit > implicit. Adding "any of" costs nothing and removes a potential misreading.

**Action:** Land — change `"Also trigger on phrases like X, Y, Z"` → `"Also trigger when any of these phrases appear: X, Y, Z"`.

---

### Finding 2 — "constraint overload in description" (severity 4) → ✅ partial fix worth landing

**Linter's take:** "Numerous constraints... may overwhelm the model... break into smaller clearly prioritized steps."

**My take:** Partly valid, partly category-error.
- **Valid:** The description is 124 words; framework recommends ~100. Tightening helps.
- **Category error:** The three constraints the linter flags (*no deferral*, *same PR*, *three sequential audits*) are not overlapping — they're the **three distinct things this skill does**. Compressing them out of the description doesn't help triggering; it makes the description ambiguous about what the skill is for. And "break into smaller prioritized steps" is exactly what the **body** does — `## Operating principle: no deferral` is its own section, and the three phases each have their own H3.

The linter is applying a generic "long-instruction = bad" template, but this is a workflow skill whose description has to convey three orthogonal claims (when, what, why-it-matters) to a model deciding whether to invoke it.

**Action:** Tighten the description from 124 → ~95 words by trimming the task #204 anecdote out of the description (it lives in the body's worked-example section anyway). Keep the three core claims. Don't break them up further.

---

### Finding 3 — "deeply nested decision tree" (severity 4) → ⚠️ disagree with diagnosis, but useful suggestion

**Linter's take:** "Sequential phases and their substeps create a deeply nested decision tree, making it difficult for the model to track progress."

**My take:** The diagnosis is wrong. The skill is **linear**, not branching:

```
Phase 1 → land all → Phase 2 (loop until 2 clean rounds) → land all → Phase 3 → land all → verify
```

That's a sequence with one bounded loop, not a decision tree. A "deep decision tree" would be: *"if A and B then X; if A and not B then Y; if not A and C then..."* — none of that exists here. There are no branches in this skill; every path leads to "land everything and continue".

But the linter's *suggestion* — "use a numbered list or flowchart to make sequence explicit" — does have value, just not for the stated reason. A 5-line *quick-flow* at the top would help a model orient before reading the dense body. Not because the structure is wrong, but because a TL;DR is always helpful in a 266-line file.

**Action:** Add a `## Quick flow` block at the top showing the linear sequence. Don't restructure the body — the body is correctly linear already.

---

### Finding 4 — "'next iteration' is ambiguous" (severity 2) → ✅ trivial fix worth landing

**My take:** Hair-splitting, but harmless to fix. "Next iteration" in SWE context means "future sprint/PR/cycle" — unambiguous in practice. But explicit is better than implicit, and the replacement is one word.

**Action:** Land — `"don't promise 'next iteration'"` → `"don't promise a future PR / sprint"`.

---

### Finding 5 — "no guidance when both spec and impl are wrong" (severity 2) → ✅ real gap, worth fixing

**My take:** This is the most useful finding. Phase 1's *"best outcome independent of either side"* framing implicitly covers the both-wrong case ("sometimes it's a totally different thing"), but the skill doesn't tell the model **how to handle that case differently** from routine drift.

In practice, when both sides are wrong, the right move differs materially:
- Routine drift → land the fix unilaterally with "best outcome" justification.
- **Both-wrong case → escalate to user via `AskUserQuestion`** because it's a real design decision, not a routine drift fix. Then land the new design in both spec and impl together, plus tests proving the new design.

The skill currently lumps these together. Splitting them out is real value.

**Action:** Land — add a paragraph to Phase 1 covering this case explicitly.

---

### Finding 6 — "missing error handling for absent spec/impl files" (severity 2) → ⚠️ mostly hypothetical, but one defensive line worth adding

**My take:** Mostly hypothetical because CLAUDE.md step 3 ("Review the cited spec sections") already requires the spec to exist before implementation starts. If the spec is missing, the workflow already broke upstream — this isn't an audit-time problem.

The one real case worth covering: a brand-new component being implemented before its spec exists (a workflow violation, but it happens). The right response there is to **surface it and halt the audit**, not to try to audit against a non-existent spec.

**Action:** Land — add one sentence to Phase 1 saying "if the cited spec is missing, surface that to the user and halt the audit; an unspecced module is a planning failure, not an audit task."

---

## Summary

| # | Worth landing? | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Ambiguous trigger phrases | ✅ Yes (small) | Add "any of these" |
| 2. Constraint overload in description | ✅ Yes (partial) | Trim to ~95 words; keep the three claims |
| 3. Deep decision tree | ⚠️ Disagree with diagnosis | But add a Quick-flow TL;DR |
| 4. "Next iteration" ambiguous | ✅ Yes (trivial) | One-word replacement |
| 5. Both-spec-and-impl-wrong gap | ✅ Yes (real) | Add paragraph to Phase 1 |
| 6. Missing-file error handling | ⚠️ Mostly hypothetical | One defensive sentence |

The high-severity findings (1, 2, 3) are real-but-overcalibrated — the linter is right that those areas have *something* to improve, but its suggested fixes are partly generic-template advice that doesn't fit this kind of workflow skill. The medium-severity findings (4, 5, 6) are smaller in claim but actually more accurate — finding 5 in particular is a real gap.

Want me to land the six fixes I marked ✅? They're a total of ~20 lines of changes, no restructuring.

29:7. **MANDATORY:** Invoke the `post-implementation-audit` skill (`.claude/skills/post-implementation-audit/SKILL.md`). It runs three sequential audits on the new code — (a) spec/implementation drift with "best outcome" recommendations, (b) iterative "anything else missing?" passes (minimum two rounds), (c) test-coverage audit across every test type the workspace uses (unit, snapshot, proptest, mutation, doc, spec-conformance, llvm-cov). **Every finding lands in _this_ PR — no deferral, no follow-up issues.** This step exists because initial implementations consistently ship spec drift and silent contract violations that take 3+ review round-trips to surface; the audit collapses them into one. **Draft commit message** (will use after xci + rebase):

> feat(components): implement Highlight agnostic core (#204)
>
> Closes #204
>
> - Add `utility::highlight` module to `ars-components` (Props, MatchStrategy, HighlightChunk, Part, Api, highlight_chunks) gated on the new `i18n` feature
> - Three match strategies (Contains, StartsWith, Fuzzy) with TR21 Unicode case folding via new `ars_i18n::case_fold` helper (`ß ↔ ss ↔ SS`, Turkic `İ ↔ i`, Greek final-sigma collapse)
> - Multi-query overlapping/adjacent range merging in the core (adapters never see back-to-back highlighted chunks)
> - 41 unit tests + 9 insta snapshots + proptest invariants + spec-conformance test
> - 0 missed mutations (`cargo mutants`), 99.91% line coverage, all clippy/doc/spec gates clean
> - Adapter feature wiring: `ars-leptos/icu4x` and `ars-dioxus/icu4x` now propagate `ars-components/i18n`
> - Spec updates: core, Leptos adapter, Dioxus adapter, foundation/10 template parametric-anatomy section
> - Add `post-implementation-audit` skill + mandatory CLAUDE.md step 7 codifying the audit flow this PR was driven through
>
> Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>

I'll proceed with the rebase + commit + push when xci completes.

{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfeat(components): implement Highlight agnostic core\n\nCloses #204\n\nAdd the framework-agnostic `Highlight` utility component to\n`ars-components::utility::highlight`. Stateless text-substring matcher\nwith three strategies (Contains / StartsWith / Fuzzy), TR21 Unicode case\nfolding via the new `ars_i18n::case_fold` helper (so `ß ↔ ss ↔ SS`,\nTurkic `İ ↔ i`, Greek final-sigma collapse, and CLDR-supplied tailored\nmappings work in both directions), and multi-query merging that\nconsolidates overlapping and adjacent ranges in the core so adapters\nnever see back-to-back highlighted chunks.\n\nAPI surface (matches the stateless-utility template):\n\n- `Props` (Default, builder, query/text/ignore_case/match_strategy)\n- `MatchStrategy` (Contains default per Ark UI parity)\n- `HighlightChunk<'a>` (Copy, Eq, Hash; borrows from Props.text)\n- `Part::Root` + `Api` (root_attrs sets dir=\"auto\"; chunk_attrs(bool)\n  for the parametric per-chunk slot; ConnectApi dispatch on Root)\n- Standalone `highlight_chunks(&Props, &Locale)` per spec §1.2\n\nBuild wiring:\n\n- New `ars-components/i18n = [\"ars-i18n/icu4x\"]` feature; the\n  `utility::highlight` module is `#[cfg(feature = \"i18n\")]` because the\n  locale-aware contract on `ignore_case = true` requires ICU4X.\n- `ars-leptos/icu4x` and `ars-dioxus/icu4x` now propagate\n  `ars-components/i18n` so adapter builds with icu4x can use Highlight.\n- Added `ars_i18n::case_fold` (gated on `icu4x`); wraps\n  `CaseMapper::fold_string` / `fold_turkic_string` based on locale.\n\nTests:\n\n- 41 unit tests covering every strategy, every edge case, full\n  ignore_case matrix, ß/SS/Turkic three-direction equivalence, fold\n  partial-match defenses, multi-query overlap + full-contain merging\n  branches, and Api accessor positive/negative directions\n- 9 insta snapshots (root, chunk attrs ×2, chunks for each strategy +\n  multi-query + Turkic + German eszett)\n- 1 inline doc test with the canonical usage example\n- 1 proptest invariant block (roundtrip, no-adjacent-highlighted,\n  no-empty-chunks, empty-edge-case) over 8 locales\n- 1 spec-conformance test asserting Part enum matches anatomy table\n- 4 new ars_i18n::case_fold tests (eszett, sigma, Turkic, idempotence)\n\nVerification: `cargo xci` clean across all 20 steps (fmt, check,\nclippy, unit, i18n-browser, dom-browser, release, integration,\nadapter, adapter-parity, coverage, snapshot-count,\nspec-compile-snippets, error-variant-coverage, 5 feature matrices,\nmutual-exclusion). `cargo mutants -p ars-components -f\ncrates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs` reports 57/57 caught,\n0 missed. Line coverage 99.91% (1 line is a defensive\nzero-length-highlight guard already documented as an equivalent\nmutation in `.cargo/mutants.toml`).\n\nSpec updates (same PR — no deferral):\n\n- `spec/components/utility/highlight.md`: complete rewrite of §1.2\n  (Connect / API with Part + Api alongside the standalone function),\n  §2 anatomy table annotates Chunk as a parametric slot, §3.1 moves\n  adjacency consolidation from \"adapters SHOULD\" to \"core does it\",\n  §3.2 says core emits dir=\"auto\", §4 adapter example uses\n  Api::root_attrs/chunk_attrs spread, §1.1 adds Default/Eq/Hash\n  derives and the builder pattern, §1.3 documents the i18n feature\n  gate, X1/X2 add Eq/Copy/Hash to MatchStrategy and HighlightChunk,\n  X3 softens the unsourced screen-reader claim.\n- `spec/leptos-components/utility/highlight.md` and\n  `spec/dioxus-components/utility/highlight.md`: §5 attr-merge tables\n  now describe Api::root_attrs/chunk_attrs and dir=\"auto\" as\n  core-owned, §23 framework-specific behaviour aligned to the new\n  core, §24 canonical implementation sketches use Api spread and\n  pass &locale, §27 a11y notes reflect core-side consolidation.\n- `spec/foundation/10-component-spec-template.md`: new §4.2\n  subsection documenting the \"static vs parametric anatomy slot\"\n  convention (the Highlight chunk being the canonical example).\n\nCodify the audit flow:\n\n- New `.agents/skills/post-implementation-audit/SKILL.md` (297 lines):\n  three sequential phases — (a) spec/impl drift audit with\n  \"best outcome\" recommendations that disregard either side as\n  default, (b) iterative \"anything else missing?\" passes until two\n  consecutive clean rounds, (c) test-coverage audit across every\n  test type (unit, snapshot, proptest, mutation, doc,\n  spec-conformance, llvm-cov + xtask wasm). Every finding lands in\n  the same PR — no deferral. Includes the both-spec-and-impl-wrong\n  escalation case (the eszett finding on this very PR), missing-file\n  precondition, anti-patterns, and the task #204 worked example.\n- `AGENTS.md` step 7: new MANDATORY step invoking the\n  post-implementation-audit skill before user review.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -10\necho \"--- commit result ---\"\ngit log --oneline -1 HEAD",
  "description": "Commit all staged changes",
  "timeout": 60000
}

{
  "body": "**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Consume full folded chars in fuzzy matches**\n\nWhen `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` runs with `ignore_case = true`, this check only consumes one query character even if the current source character folds to multiple characters. For example, with German locale, `text = \"\u00df\"` and `query = [\"ss\"]`, the folded query has two `s` characters, but the single `\u00df` source character consumes only the first one and the remaining `s` causes the local range to be discarded. That violates the spec's case-folding contract that `\"\u00df\"`, `\"ss\"`, and `\"SS\"` match each other, making fuzzy matching asymmetric.\n\nUseful? React with \ud83d\udc4d\u00a0/ \ud83d\udc4e.",
  "id": 3263357790,
  "in_reply_to": null,
  "line": 515,
  "path": "crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs"
}
---
{
  "body": "**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Restore Eq on Highlight props**\n\nThe component spec publishes `Highlight` `Props` with an `Eq` implementation, and all four fields already support `Eq`, but this derive list only exposes `PartialEq`. Consumers that rely on the spec'd `Props: Eq` bound for generic equality/conformance helpers will fail to compile once they enable this component, so the implementation should derive `Eq` as part of the public API contract.\n\nUseful? React with \ud83d\udc4d\u00a0/ \ud83d\udc4e.",
  "id": 3263357792,
  "in_reply_to": null,
  "line": 34,
  "path": "crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs"
}
---
{
  "body": "**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Include overlapping occurrences in contains matches**\n\nFor a single query that overlaps itself, `str::match_indices` only returns non-overlapping matches, so cases like `text = \"banana\"`, `query = [\"ana\"]` highlight only the first `ana` and leave the final `na` unhighlighted. The spec says `Contains` highlights every occurrence and then merges overlapping ranges, so the scan needs to advance by character boundaries rather than relying on the non-overlapping iterator.\n\nUseful? React with \ud83d\udc4d\u00a0/ \ud83d\udc4e.",
  "id": 3263357793,
  "in_reply_to": null,
  "line": 456,
  "path": "crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs"
}

{
  "body": "**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Enable i18n in nightly Highlight proptests**\n\nThis new invariant test is behind `#[cfg(feature = \"i18n\")]`, but I checked `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` and the extended-proptest job runs `cargo test -p ars-core -p ars-components -- --ignored proptest` without `--features i18n` (Cargo's help lists feature activation under `--features`). Since `ars-components` defaults only to `std`, the Highlight proptest is not compiled or run in nightly CI, leaving the new case-folding/range invariants unexercised unless the workflow enables the feature.\n\nUseful? React with \ud83d\udc4d\u00a0/ \ud83d\udc4e.",
  "id": 3263357795,

{
  "body": "**<sub><sub>![P2 Badge](https://img.shields.io/badge/P2-yellow?style=flat)</sub></sub>  Map partial fold boundaries to source chars**\n\nThis byte map records every byte produced by a folded character as the source character's start offset, so a match that ends inside a case-fold expansion can exclude the source character that contributed the matched byte. For example, `StartsWith` with German `text = \"Stra\u00dfe\"` and `query = [\"stras\"]` matches the folded prefix `stras`, but `byte_map[end]` points to the start of `\u00df`, causing the highlighted chunk to be only `\"Stra\"`; single-byte prefixes such as `text = \"\u00df\"`, `query = [\"s\"]` can even be dropped as a zero-length range. End boundaries inside an expansion need to map to the contributing source character's end.\n\nUseful? React with \ud83d\udc4d\u00a0/ \ud83d\udc4e.",
  "id": 3263357797,
  "in_reply_to": null,

399	/// Build a normalised form of `text` (lowercased when `ignore_case`) plus a
400	/// byte-map from normalised byte indices back to original byte indices.
401	///
402	/// The map has length `normalised.len() + 1`. For each normalised byte `i`
403	/// produced from original char starting at `orig_byte`, `byte_map[i] ==
404	/// orig_byte`. The trailing entry `byte_map[normalised.len()] == text.len()`
405	/// is a sentinel so callers can read the end index of a match without
406	/// bounds-special-casing.
407	///
408	/// When `!ignore_case`, the normalised text is `text` verbatim and the map
409	/// is the identity `0..=text.len()` (each byte trivially maps to itself).
410	fn build_normalised(text: &str, ignore_case: bool, locale: &Locale) -> (String, Vec<usize>) {
411	    if !ignore_case {
412	        let map: Vec<usize> = (0..=text.len()).collect();
413	
414	        return (text.to_owned(), map);
415	    }
416	
417	    let mut normalised = String::with_capacity(text.len());
418	    let mut byte_map = Vec::with_capacity(text.len() + 1);
419	
420	    for (orig_byte, ch) in text.char_indices() {
421	        let mut buf = [0u8; 4];
422	
423	        let ch_str = ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf);
424	
425	        let folded = case_fold(ch_str, locale);
426	
427	        for _ in 0..folded.len() {
428	            byte_map.push(orig_byte);
429	        }
430	
431	        normalised.push_str(&folded);
432	    }
433	
434	    byte_map.push(text.len());
435	
436	    (normalised, byte_map)
437	}
438	
439	/// Unicode case fold for case-insensitive comparison. Delegates to
440	/// [`ars_i18n::case_fold`] which wraps ICU4X
441	/// `CaseMapper::fold_string` (or `fold_turkic_string` under Turkic
442	/// locales). The fold form — not lowercase — is the right primitive for
443	/// matching: it expands `ß → ss`, collapses Greek final sigma into the
444	/// medial form, and (under Turkic) folds `İ ↔ i` / `I ↔ ı`. This is what
445	/// the spec's `ignore_case = true` contract requires.
446	fn case_fold(text: &str, locale: &Locale) -> String {
447	    ars_i18n::case_fold(text, locale)
448	}
449	
450	fn push_contains_matches(
451	    normalised_text: &str,
452	    normalised_query: &str,
453	    byte_map: &[usize],
454	    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,
455	) {
456	    for (start, _) in normalised_text.match_indices(normalised_query) {
457	        let end = start + normalised_query.len();
458	        let orig_start = byte_map[start];
459	        let orig_end = byte_map[end];
460	
461	        if orig_start < orig_end {
462	            out.push((orig_start, orig_end));
463	        }
464	    }
465	}
466	
467	fn push_starts_with_match(
468	    normalised_text: &str,
469	    normalised_query: &str,
470	    byte_map: &[usize],
471	    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,
472	) {
473	    if normalised_text.starts_with(normalised_query) {
474	        let end = normalised_query.len();
475	        let orig_end = byte_map[end];
476	
477	        if orig_end > 0 {
478	            out.push((0, orig_end));
479	        }
480	    }
481	}
482	
483	/// Walk the original text char-by-char, lowercasing each char individually
484	/// when needed, and consume the query in order. Each matched original-text
485	/// character contributes a one-char range to `out`. Fuzzy matching collapses
486	/// to no match (zero ranges pushed) when any query char cannot be located.
487	fn push_fuzzy_matches(
488	    original_text: &str,
489	    normalised_query: &str,
490	    ignore_case: bool,
491	    locale: &Locale,
492	    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,
493	) {
494	    let mut query_iter = normalised_query.chars().peekable();
495	    let mut local = Vec::new();
496	
497	    for (orig_byte, ch) in original_text.char_indices() {
498	        let Some(&want) = query_iter.peek() else {
499	            break;
500	        };
501	
502	        let normalised_ch_string = if ignore_case {
503	            let mut buf = [0u8; 4];
504	            let ch_str = ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf);
505	
506	            case_fold(ch_str, locale)
507	        } else {
508	            let mut s = String::new();
509	
510	            s.push(ch);
511	
512	            s
513	        };
514	
515	        if normalised_ch_string.chars().any(|c| c == want) {
516	            local.push((orig_byte, orig_byte + ch.len_utf8()));
517	
518	            query_iter.next();
519	        }

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Build a normalised form of `text` (lowercased when `ignore_case`) plus a\n/// byte-map from normalised byte indices back to original byte indices.\n///\n/// The map has length `normalised.len() + 1`. For each normalised byte `i`\n/// produced from original char starting at `orig_byte`, `byte_map[i] ==\n/// orig_byte`. The trailing entry `byte_map[normalised.len()] == text.len()`\n/// is a sentinel so callers can read the end index of a match without\n/// bounds-special-casing.\n///\n/// When `!ignore_case`, the normalised text is `text` verbatim and the map\n/// is the identity `0..=text.len()` (each byte trivially maps to itself).\nfn build_normalised(text: &str, ignore_case: bool, locale: &Locale) -> (String, Vec<usize>) {\n    if !ignore_case {\n        let map: Vec<usize> = (0..=text.len()).collect();\n\n        return (text.to_owned(), map);\n    }\n\n    let mut normalised = String::with_capacity(text.len());\n    let mut byte_map = Vec::with_capacity(text.len() + 1);\n\n    for (orig_byte, ch) in text.char_indices() {\n        let mut buf = [0u8; 4];\n\n        let ch_str = ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf);\n\n        let folded = case_fold(ch_str, locale);\n\n        for _ in 0..folded.len() {\n            byte_map.push(orig_byte);\n        }\n\n        normalised.push_str(&folded);\n    }\n\n    byte_map.push(text.len());\n\n    (normalised, byte_map)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Normalised text plus a byte-level mapping back to the original source\n/// text. Each normalised byte `i` has two map entries:\n///\n/// - `source_start[i]` — original byte index where the source character\n///   that produced normalised byte `i` *starts*.\n/// - `source_end[i]` — original byte index where that source character\n///   *ends* (i.e., `source_start + char.len_utf8()`).\n///\n/// Both arrays are aligned: `source_start.len() == source_end.len() ==\n/// normalised.len()`. For a normalised match `[a, b)` we always map to\n/// the source range `[source_start[a], source_end[b - 1]]`, even when\n/// `a` or `b` falls *inside* a case-fold expansion (e.g. `ß → ss`).\n/// Using `source_end[b - 1]` rather than `source_start[b]` is what lets\n/// a match that ends mid-expansion still highlight the full source\n/// character that contributed the matched bytes — without this, queries\n/// like `\"stras\"` against text `\"Straße\"` would highlight only the\n/// `\"Stra\"` prefix and drop the `ß` that contributed the trailing `s`.\nstruct Normalised {\n    text: String,\n    source_start: Vec<usize>,\n    source_end: Vec<usize>,\n}\n\nimpl Normalised {\n    /// Build the normalised view of `text`. When `!ignore_case`, the\n    /// normalised text is the original verbatim and the maps are the\n    /// per-byte identity.\n    fn build(text: &str, ignore_case: bool, locale: &Locale) -> Self {\n        if !ignore_case {\n            let source_start: Vec<usize> = (0..text.len()).collect();\n            let source_end: Vec<usize> = (1..=text.len()).collect();\n            return Self {\n                text: text.to_owned(),\n                source_start,\n                source_end,\n            };\n        }\n\n        let mut normalised = String::with_capacity(text.len());\n        let mut source_start = Vec::with_capacity(text.len());\n        let mut source_end = Vec::with_capacity(text.len());\n\n        for (orig_byte, ch) in text.char_indices() {\n            let mut buf = [0u8; 4];\n            let ch_str = ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf);\n            let folded = case_fold(ch_str, locale);\n            let end_of_source_char = orig_byte + ch.len_utf8();\n\n            for _ in 0..folded.len() {\n                source_start.push(orig_byte);\n                source_end.push(end_of_source_char);\n            }\n\n            normalised.push_str(&folded);\n        }\n\n        Self {\n            text: normalised,\n            source_start,\n            source_end,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Map a normalised half-open range `[start_norm, end_norm)` to the\n    /// corresponding original-text half-open range. `end_norm` must be\n    /// strictly greater than `start_norm` (the caller filters empty\n    /// matches before calling).\n    fn map_range(&self, start_norm: usize, end_norm: usize) -> (usize, usize) {\n        debug_assert!(end_norm > start_norm, \"Normalised::map_range requires a non-empty range\");\n        (self.source_start[start_norm], self.source_end[end_norm - 1])\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "fn push_contains_matches(\n    normalised_text: &str,\n    normalised_query: &str,\n    byte_map: &[usize],\n    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,\n) {\n    for (start, _) in normalised_text.match_indices(normalised_query) {\n        let end = start + normalised_query.len();\n        let orig_start = byte_map[start];\n        let orig_end = byte_map[end];\n\n        if orig_start < orig_end {\n            out.push((orig_start, orig_end));\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nfn push_starts_with_match(\n    normalised_text: &str,\n    normalised_query: &str,\n    byte_map: &[usize],\n    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,\n) {\n    if normalised_text.starts_with(normalised_query) {\n        let end = normalised_query.len();\n        let orig_end = byte_map[end];\n\n        if orig_end > 0 {\n            out.push((0, orig_end));\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Push every occurrence of `normalised_query` inside `normalised.text`,\n/// **including overlapping ones** (`str::match_indices` only yields the\n/// non-overlapping ones, which would miss `\"ana\"` at position 3 of\n/// `\"banana\"`). Advances by one character past each match's start to\n/// hit the next overlapping occurrence.\nfn push_contains_matches(\n    normalised: &Normalised,\n    normalised_query: &str,\n    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,\n) {\n    if normalised_query.is_empty() {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    let haystack = normalised.text.as_str();\n    let mut search_start = 0;\n\n    while let Some(rel) = haystack[search_start..].find(normalised_query) {\n        let start = search_start + rel;\n        let end = start + normalised_query.len();\n        let (orig_start, orig_end) = normalised.map_range(start, end);\n\n        out.push((orig_start, orig_end));\n\n        // Advance past one character so the next iteration can find\n        // overlapping matches. UTF-8 guarantees that `find` returned a\n        // position on a char boundary, so `chars().next()` is sound.\n        let advance = haystack[start..]\n            .chars()\n            .next()\n            .map_or(1, char::len_utf8);\n        search_start = start + advance;\n    }\n}\n\nfn push_starts_with_match(\n    normalised: &Normalised,\n    normalised_query: &str,\n    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,\n) {\n    if normalised_query.is_empty() {\n        return;\n    }\n\n    if normalised.text.starts_with(normalised_query) {\n        let end = normalised_query.len();\n        let (_, orig_end) = normalised.map_range(0, end);\n        out.push((0, orig_end));\n    }\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/.t3/worktrees/ars-ui/t3code-7f73cfa1/crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Walk the original text char-by-char, lowercasing each char individually\n/// when needed, and consume the query in order. Each matched original-text\n/// character contributes a one-char range to `out`. Fuzzy matching collapses\n/// to no match (zero ranges pushed) when any query char cannot be located.\nfn push_fuzzy_matches(\n    original_text: &str,\n    normalised_query: &str,\n    ignore_case: bool,\n    locale: &Locale,\n    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,\n) {\n    let mut query_iter = normalised_query.chars().peekable();\n    let mut local = Vec::new();\n\n    for (orig_byte, ch) in original_text.char_indices() {\n        let Some(&want) = query_iter.peek() else {\n            break;\n        };\n\n        let normalised_ch_string = if ignore_case {\n            let mut buf = [0u8; 4];\n            let ch_str = ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf);\n\n            case_fold(ch_str, locale)\n        } else {\n            let mut s = String::new();\n\n            s.push(ch);\n\n            s\n        };\n\n        if normalised_ch_string.chars().any(|c| c == want) {\n            local.push((orig_byte, orig_byte + ch.len_utf8()));\n\n            query_iter.next();\n        }\n    }\n\n    if query_iter.peek().is_none() {\n        out.extend(local);\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Walk the original text char-by-char and consume the query in order.\n/// Each source character that contributes at least one matched query\n/// character produces a one-char highlighted range. Fuzzy matching is\n/// all-or-nothing — if any query character cannot be located in order,\n/// the function emits zero ranges for this query.\n///\n/// **Multi-char fold expansions**: a single source char can fold to\n/// multiple chars (e.g. `ß → ss` under German), so we consume *as many*\n/// query characters as match the source char's folded expansion *in\n/// order*. Without this, `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` with `query=[\"ss\"]` and\n/// `text=\"ß\"` would consume only the first `s` from the query, fail on\n/// the second, and emit no match — violating the spec's eszett\n/// equivalence contract.\nfn push_fuzzy_matches(\n    original_text: &str,\n    normalised_query: &str,\n    ignore_case: bool,\n    locale: &Locale,\n    out: &mut Vec<(usize, usize)>,\n) {\n    let mut query_iter = normalised_query.chars().peekable();\n    let mut local = Vec::new();\n\n    for (orig_byte, ch) in original_text.char_indices() {\n        if query_iter.peek().is_none() {\n            break;\n        }\n\n        let normalised_ch_string = if ignore_case {\n            let mut buf = [0u8; 4];\n            let ch_str = ch.encode_utf8(&mut buf);\n            case_fold(ch_str, locale)\n        } else {\n            let mut s = String::new();\n            s.push(ch);\n            s\n        };\n\n        // Consume every leading query character that appears, in order,\n        // in this source char's folded expansion. For single-char folds\n        // (the common case) this consumes at most one query char; for\n        // expansions like `ß → ss` it may consume two or more.\n        let mut consumed_any = false;\n        for folded_char in normalised_ch_string.chars() {\n            match query_iter.peek() {\n                Some(&want) if want == folded_char => {\n                    query_iter.next();\n                    consumed_any = true;\n                }\n                _ => {}\n            }\n        }\n\n        if consumed_any {\n            local.push((orig_byte, orig_byte + ch.len_utf8()));\n        }\n    }\n\n    if query_iter.peek().is_none() {\n        out.extend(local);\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "    #[test]\n    fn fold_expansion_partial_match_does_not_emit_partial_char_highlight() {\n        // Branch coverage for the byte-map zero-length-range filter in\n        // both `push_contains_matches` (orig_start < orig_end) and\n        // `push_starts_with_match` (orig_end > 0). Under en-US, \"İ\"\n        // folds to \"i\\u{307}\" (i + combining dot above, 3 bytes). A\n        // query \"i\" matches the first folded byte but maps to a\n        // zero-length original range — the implementation must NOT emit\n        // a partial-character highlight, because rendering only part of\n        // a code point would corrupt the displayed text.\n        for strategy in [MatchStrategy::Contains, MatchStrategy::StartsWith] {\n            let props = Props::new()\n                .query([\"i\"])\n                .text(\"İa\")\n                .match_strategy(strategy);\n\n            let chunks = highlight_chunks(&props, &en_us());\n\n            let highlighted: Vec<&str> = chunks\n                .iter()\n                .filter(|c| c.highlighted)\n                .map(|c| c.text)\n                .collect();\n\n            assert!(\n                highlighted.is_empty(),\n                \"{strategy:?}: must not emit partial-char highlight, got {highlighted:?}\"\n            );\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "    #[test]\n    fn fold_expansion_partial_match_highlights_full_source_char() {\n        // Under en-US (non-Turkic), `İ` case-folds to `i\\u{307}` (i +\n        // combining dot above, 3 bytes). A query of `\"i\"` matches the\n        // leading byte of that expansion. The implementation must\n        // highlight the **entire** source character `İ` (not nothing,\n        // and not just the leading byte), because rendering only part\n        // of a code point would corrupt the displayed text. Verified\n        // for both Contains and StartsWith — the byte-map's\n        // `source_end` mapping is what lets the match extend to the\n        // source char's full UTF-8 span.\n        for strategy in [MatchStrategy::Contains, MatchStrategy::StartsWith] {\n            let props = Props::new()\n                .query([\"i\"])\n                .text(\"İa\")\n                .match_strategy(strategy);\n\n            let chunks = highlight_chunks(&props, &en_us());\n\n            let highlighted: Vec<&str> = chunks\n                .iter()\n                .filter(|c| c.highlighted)\n                .map(|c| c.text)\n                .collect();\n\n            assert_eq!(\n                highlighted,\n                vec![\"İ\"],\n                \"{strategy:?}: must highlight the full `İ`, got {highlighted:?}\"\n            );\n        }\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn contains_strategy_finds_overlapping_occurrences() {\n        // `str::match_indices` only yields non-overlapping matches, so\n        // a naive implementation would highlight only the first `ana`\n        // in `banana` and miss the second. The spec says Contains\n        // highlights **every** occurrence then merges overlaps, so the\n        // expected result is one merged highlight covering `anana`.\n        let props = Props::new().query([\"ana\"]).text(\"banana\");\n\n        let chunks = highlight_chunks(&props, &en_us());\n\n        assert_eq!(\n            chunks,\n            vec![\n                HighlightChunk {\n                    text: \"b\",\n                    highlighted: false,\n                },\n                HighlightChunk {\n                    text: \"anana\",\n                    highlighted: true,\n                },\n            ]\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn starts_with_extends_match_to_full_fold_expansion_source_char() {\n        // German `Straße` folds to `strasse`. Query `stras` matches\n        // the folded prefix, with its final byte coming from the\n        // `ß`'s fold expansion. The highlighted span must extend to\n        // cover the full `ß` so the rendered output is `Straß`, not\n        // `Stra` (which would slice off the ß mid-codepoint).\n        let props = Props::new()\n            .query([\"stras\"])\n            .text(\"Straße\")\n            .match_strategy(MatchStrategy::StartsWith);\n\n        let chunks = highlight_chunks(&props, &german());\n\n        assert_eq!(\n            chunks,\n            vec![\n                HighlightChunk {\n                    text: \"Straß\",\n                    highlighted: true,\n                },\n                HighlightChunk {\n                    text: \"e\",\n                    highlighted: false,\n                },\n            ]\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn fuzzy_consumes_full_folded_expansion_for_eszett() {\n        // Under German, `ß` folds to `ss`. A fuzzy query of `ss`\n        // against text `ß` must consume **both** query characters\n        // against the single source char's folded expansion, then\n        // emit a one-char highlight for the `ß` itself. Without\n        // multi-char consumption the second `s` is left in the query,\n        // fuzzy's all-or-nothing rule kicks in, and `ß` ↔ `ss`\n        // equivalence breaks asymmetrically with the other strategies.\n        let props = Props::new()\n            .query([\"ss\"])\n            .text(\"ß\")\n            .match_strategy(MatchStrategy::Fuzzy);\n\n        let chunks = highlight_chunks(&props, &german());\n\n        assert_eq!(\n            chunks,\n            vec![HighlightChunk {\n                text: \"ß\",\n                highlighted: true,\n            }]\n        );\n    }"
}

159:/// When multiple queries (or fuzzy hits) produce overlapping or adjacent
170:/// - `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` with any missing query character → that query
202:- **Adjacent / overlapping chunk consolidation is done in the agnostic core.** `highlight_chunks` merges overlapping and adjacent ranges before returning, so adapters never see back-to-back `<mark>` elements covering contiguous text and screen readers never get a stutter of "highlighted, highlighted, highlighted" for adjacent fuzzy hits. Adapters MAY still provide a summary announcement (e.g., "N characters matched") for high-cardinality fuzzy results, as a layered enhancement.
262:| Fuzzy matching                 | Yes (`MatchStrategy::Fuzzy`)      | --     |
265:| Adjacent/overlap merge in core | Yes                               | --     |
273:- **Divergences:** ars-ui uses `MatchStrategy` enum (Contains/StartsWith/Fuzzy) while Ark uses `exactMatch`/`matchAll` booleans. ars-ui adds locale-aware Unicode case folding, fuzzy matching, core-side adjacency merging, and a BiDi-safe root via `dir="auto"`.
```

55	pub enum MatchStrategy {
56	    /// Highlight every occurrence of the query within the text. Default.
57	    #[default]
58	    Contains,
59	    /// Highlight only the leading run of the text when it starts with the
60	    /// query (after case folding, if `ignore_case` is `true`).

Default.\n    #[default]\n    Contains,\n    /// Highlight only the leading run of the text when it starts with the\n    /// query (after case folding, if `ignore_case` is `true`).\n    StartsWith,\n    /// Subsequence match: highlight each individual query character within\n    /// the text in order, allowing arbitrary gaps. All query characters\n    /// must be present; if any character is missing, no fuzzy ranges are\n    /// emitted for that query (all-or-nothing).\n    Fuzzy,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// The strategy used to match the query against the text.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]\npub enum MatchStrategy {\n    /// Highlight every occurrence of the query within the text, including\n    /// overlapping ones (e.g., `query = \"ana\"` against `text = \"banana\"`\n    /// produces two matches at byte offsets 1 and 3, which then merge\n    /// into a single highlighted span covering `\"anana\"`). Default.\n    #[default]\n    Contains,\n    /// Highlight only the leading run of the text when it starts with the\n    /// query (after case folding, if `ignore_case` is `true`). When the\n    /// matched prefix ends inside a case-fold expansion (e.g.,\n    /// `query = \"stras\"` against German `text = \"Straße\"` where `ß`\n    /// folds to `ss`), the highlighted span extends to cover the full\n    /// source character that contributed the matched fold bytes.\n    StartsWith,\n    /// Subsequence match: highlight each individual query character within\n    /// the text in order, allowing arbitrary gaps. All query characters\n    /// must be present; if any character is missing, no fuzzy ranges are\n    /// emitted for that query (all-or-nothing). When a source character's\n    /// case-fold expansion contains multiple characters (e.g., `ß → ss`\n    /// under German), the matcher consumes **all** consecutive query\n    /// characters that appear in that expansion in order, so a fuzzy\n    /// query of `\"ss\"` matches text `\"ß\"` symmetrically with the\n    /// equivalence Contains delivers.\n    Fuzzy,\n}"
}

312	/// When multiple queries (or fuzzy hits) produce overlapping **or
313	/// adjacent** ranges, they are merged into a single highlighted chunk so
314	/// adapters never render back-to-back `<mark>` elements covering
315	/// contiguous text. This satisfies spec §3.1's adjacency-consolidation
316	/// requirement in the core, leaving adapters free to layer only
317	/// genuinely additional a11y behaviour (e.g., summary announcements for
318	/// high-cardinality fuzzy results) on top.
319	///
320	/// # Edge cases
321	///
322	/// - Empty `query`, or `query` containing only empty strings → a single
323	///   non-highlighted chunk wrapping the full text.
324	/// - Empty `text` → an empty [`Vec`] (nothing to render).
325	/// - Query longer than text → a single non-highlighted chunk.
326	/// - `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` with any query character missing from the
327	///   text → no fuzzy ranges contributed for that query (all-or-nothing).
328	#[must_use]
329	pub fn highlight_chunks<'a>(props: &'a Props, locale: &Locale) -> Vec<HighlightChunk<'a>> {
330	    let text = props.text.as_str();
331	
332	    if text.is_empty() {
333	        return Vec::new();
334	    }
335	
336	    let non_empty_queries: Vec<&str> = props
337	        .query
338	        .iter()
339	        .map(String::as_str)
```

{

  "old_string": "  # Highlight component: three equivalent mutations in the range\n  # construction / merge / chunk-emission pipeline. All three are\n  # defensive guards against zero-length original-text ranges produced\n  # when a case-fold expansion (e.g., `ß → ss`) is partially matched.\n  # No code path in the pipeline can produce a zero-length range that\n  # would survive the *next* filter, so weakening any single guard\n  # produces equivalent end-to-end behaviour (verified by output diff\n  # against the test corpus).\n  #\n  # - `< → <=` in push_contains_matches: would push (a, a) ranges; those\n  #   then get filtered by build_chunks's `end > start` check before\n  #   emitting a chunk.\n  # - `> → >=` in merge_ranges: when `end == last.1`, the body becomes\n  #   `last.1 = last.1`, a no-op.\n  # - `> → >=` in build_chunks: would push zero-length highlighted\n  #   chunks, but no upstream pipeline stage produces zero-length ranges\n  #   (push_contains_matches filters them; push_starts_with_match\n  #   requires `orig_end > 0`; push_fuzzy_matches always uses\n  #   `len_utf8() >= 1`).\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace < with <= in push_contains_matches',\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace > with >= in merge_ranges',\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace > with >= in build_chunks',",
  "new_string": "  # Highlight component: two equivalent mutations in the merge /\n  # chunk-emission pipeline. These are dead-defensive guards — no\n  # upstream stage produces zero-length ranges (post-redesign with the\n  # `Normalised::source_end` mapping, the contains/starts-with scans\n  # never push degenerate ranges, and fuzzy always uses\n  # `len_utf8() >= 1`).\n  #\n  # - `> → >=` in merge_ranges: when `end == last.1`, the body becomes\n  #   `last.1 = last.1`, a no-op.\n  # - `> → >=` in build_chunks: would push zero-length highlighted\n  #   chunks, but no upstream stage produces zero-length ranges.\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace > with >= in merge_ranges',\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace > with >= in build_chunks',\n\n  # Highlight `push_contains_matches` overlap-aware scan: the three\n  # `+ → */-` mutants on `search_start + rel`, `start + normalised_query.len()`,\n  # and `search_start = start + advance` are *caught* via test\n  # non-termination — replacing the increment with `*` or `-` either\n  # pins `search_start` at 0 (e.g., `start * 1 == start`) or panics on\n  # usize underflow inside the `find` loop, both of which take the\n  # mutated process past cargo-mutants' 300s timeout. The mutations\n  # ARE detected (timeout != missed), but reporting them as timeouts\n  # without these excludes would otherwise wedge the CI matrix on a\n  # caught-but-slow result. Same pattern as the existing typeahead /\n  # toast manager loop-step excludes above.\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace \\+ with \\* in push_contains_matches',\n  'highlight\\.rs:\\d+:\\d+: replace \\+ with - in push_contains_matches',"

```json
{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(highlight): address Codex PR review feedback\n\nFive inline review comments on #647 surfaced semantic gaps in the\ncase-fold-aware matching pipeline. Each lands here with a corresponding\ntest that pins the new behaviour.\n\n1. Map fold-expansion match boundaries to the *end* of the\n   contributing source char.\n\n   The byte-map previously recorded only the start byte of the source\n   character that produced each folded byte, so a match ending inside\n   a fold expansion (e.g. `\"stras\"` against German `\"Straße\"`, where\n   the trailing `s` comes from `ß`'s `ss` fold) collapsed `byte_map[end]`\n   to the *start* of `ß` and the highlighted span lost the `ß`\n   entirely — `\"Stra\"` instead of `\"Straß\"`.\n\n   `Normalised` now tracks two parallel maps (`source_start[i]` and\n   `source_end[i]`) and `map_range(a, b)` returns\n   `(source_start[a], source_end[b-1])`. Match ends inside a fold\n   expansion now extend to the full contributing source char.\n\n2. Contains scan covers overlapping occurrences.\n\n   `str::match_indices` only yields non-overlapping matches, so\n   `query=[\"ana\"]` against `text=\"banana\"` missed the second\n   occurrence at byte 3. The spec says Contains highlights every\n   occurrence and merges overlaps. Rewrote `push_contains_matches`\n   to scan with `find`, advancing by one character past each match's\n   start so overlapping hits surface and merge correctly into a\n   single `\"anana\"` span.\n\n3. Fuzzy consumes full folded expansions.\n\n   `MatchStrategy::Fuzzy` with `ignore_case = true` previously\n   consumed one query character per source character, so\n   `query=[\"ss\"]` against `text=\"ß\"` consumed only the first `s`\n   from the query, failed on the second, and emitted no fuzzy match\n   — asymmetric with the Contains/StartsWith behaviour for the same\n   inputs. `push_fuzzy_matches` now consumes every leading query\n   character that appears, in order, in each source character's\n   folded expansion, so the ß/ss equivalence holds across all three\n   strategies.\n\n4. Restore `Eq` on `Highlight::Props`.\n\n   The spec declares `Props: Eq` and every field already supports it,\n   but the impl's derive list shipped with only `PartialEq`,\n   breaking any downstream code that depends on the documented `Eq`\n   bound. Added `Eq` to the derive list and reworded the spec to\n   call out the manual `impl Default` (`#[derive(Default)]` cannot\n   express the non-field-default `ignore_case = true` and\n   `match_strategy = MatchStrategy::Contains` contracts).\n\n5. Enable `i18n` for the nightly Highlight proptest.\n\n   `.github/workflows/nightly.yml` ran the extended-proptest job\n   without `--features ars-components/i18n`, so the feature-gated\n   Highlight invariant proptest silently disappeared from the\n   nightly suite. Added the feature flag so the locale-aware\n   case-folding invariants are actually exercised.\n\nTest surface changes (no coverage loss):\n\n- Replaced `fold_expansion_partial_match_does_not_emit_partial_char_highlight`\n  (asserted the OLD, incorrect behaviour) with\n  `fold_expansion_partial_match_highlights_full_source_char` (asserts\n  the new correct behaviour: query \"i\" against \"İa\" highlights the\n  full \"İ\" under en-US, where `İ` folds to `i\\u{307}`).\n- Added `contains_strategy_finds_overlapping_occurrences` covering\n  the `\"ana\"`/`\"banana\"` case from review #3.\n- Added `starts_with_extends_match_to_full_fold_expansion_source_char`\n  covering the `\"stras\"`/`\"Straße\"` case from review #5.\n- Added `fuzzy_consumes_full_folded_expansion_for_eszett` covering\n  the `\"ss\"`/`\"ß\"` case from review #1.\n\nVerification:\n\n- `cargo xci`: all 20 steps pass.\n- `cargo test -p ars-components --features i18n --all-targets`:\n  1512 lib + 4 conformance pass (was 1504 + 3 in the previous push;\n  +8 from the new tests and pre-existing growth).\n- `cargo test -p ars-components --features i18n --doc utility::highlight`:\n  doc test still passes.\n- `cargo mutants -p ars-components -f\n  crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs`: 55/55 caught\n  (3 new equivalent-by-non-termination mutations on the\n  `push_contains_matches` `+` operators added to `.cargo/mutants.toml`\n  with justifications; the obsolete `< → <=` skip from before the\n  byte-map redesign is now removed).\n- `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features --exclude\n  ars-i18n -- -D warnings`: clean.\n\nSpec updates in the same commit per the no-deferral rule (CLAUDE.md\nstep 7 / post-implementation-audit skill):\n\n- `spec/components/utility/highlight.md` §1.1: rewrote the\n  `Contains`, `StartsWith`, and `Fuzzy` docstrings to describe the\n  corrected semantics (overlapping matches, full source-char\n  extension, multi-char fold consumption). Removed `Default` from\n  the Props derive list and added a doc comment explaining the\n  manual `impl Default`.\n- `crates/ars-components/src/utility/highlight.rs`:\n  `highlight_chunks` rustdoc gets the same corrected wording.\n\nCo-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5\necho \"---\"\ngit log --oneline -3 HEAD",
  "description": "Commit review fixes",
  "timeout": 120000
}

New test `starts_with_extends_match_to_full_fold_expansion_source_char` pins this.\n- `query=[\"s\"]` against `text=\"ß\"` — match `[0, 1)`. `source_end[0] = 2` (end of `ß`), so the whole `ß` highlights instead of being dropped as a zero-length range. Covered transitively by the new test plus `fold_expansion_partial_match_highlights_full_source_char` (which exercises `İ`/`i\\u{307}` for both Contains and StartsWith).\n\nThe old \"zero-length range filter\" guards in `push_contains_matches` (`orig_start < orig_end`) and `push_starts_with_match` (`orig_end > 0`) are gone — they were filtering out exactly the matches that should now produce a source-char-bounded highlight. Mutation testing recalibrated: 55/55 caught, with the now-unreachable filter excludes removed from `.cargo/mutants.toml`.\nEOF\n)\" --jq '.id' 2>&1\necho \"--- reply 5 done ---\"",
